**Proof route.** For a p-complete R-algebra `S` the absolute prismatic complex of `S` agrees with the relative one, `Δ_S ≃ Δ_{S/A}`, compatibly with Frobenius (Bhatt–Lurie Proposition 4.4.12; §4.19 for the perfect prism A).

### 6.26 p-adic Tate twists of perfectoid rings and étale cohomology of the generic fibre

Let `R` be a perfectoid ring. For integer `n ≥ 1` there is a canonical identification `Z_p(n)(R) ≃ RΓ_ét(Spec(R[1/p]), Z_p(n))`, where the right-hand side is the derived limit over `k` of `RΓ_ét(Spec(R[1/p]), μ_{p^k}^{⊗n})`. For `n = 0` there are canonical identifications `Z_p(0)(R) ≃ RΓ_ét(Spec R, Z_p) ≃ RΓ_ét(Spec((R/p)_perf), Z_p)`. The identifications are multiplicative in `n`, natural in `R`, and for `n = 1` the map is the Kummer map T_p-completion of units: on a p-complete, p-torsion-free rank-one valuation ring `V` with algebraically closed fraction field `C` it is `T_p(V^×) → T_p(C^×) → H^0(Spec C, Z_p(1))`. Moreover, as presheaves on perfectoid rings both sides are, p-completely arc-locally, concentrated in degree 0 and p-torsion-free, and the multiplication `Z_p(1)^{⊗̂n} → Z_p(n)` exhibits the target as the p-complete arc-sheafification of the source.

`R` is an arbitrary perfectoid ring; for `n ≥ 1` both sides vanish on rings of characteristic `p`. For `n ≥ 1` the comparison is with the generic fibre `Spec R[1/p]`; for `n = 0` with `Spec R`, equivalently with the special fibre. The two cases are different statements. The comparison map for `n = 1` is constructed from `Z_p(1) = lim μ_{p^n}` (§6.24), not from the cyclotomic trace; with this input the proof is purely algebraic (Bhatt–Scholze Remark 9.5). In the source proof, `(∗_b)` concerns `(G_1/p)^{⊗n} → G_n/p`, and the final sheaf check is on stalks.

(BS22, Theorem 9.4 and its proof; p. 73; BS22, Remark 9.5; p. 75.)

*Needs:* §6.8, §6.24, §6.3, §6.4, §6.25, §6.5, §6.12, PerfectoidSpaces §P4.15.2, PrismaticCohomology §0.77, PerfectoidSpaces P1.16–P1.18, P1.24–P1.25, SchemeAndStackFoundations §§2.1–2.6, SchemeAndStackFoundations §0.14, §2.15; ClassicalAdicEtaleCohomology §2.1.

**Checks.**

- At `R=O_C` with `C` algebraically closed, weight zero is `Z_p` in degree zero.
- At the perfectoid ring `R=F_p`, weight zero is `fib(0:Z_p→Z_p)=Z_p⊕Z_p[−1]`; its nonzero `H¹` prevents replacing derived fixed points by their kernel.
- At `R=O_C×O_C`, weight-zero `H⁰=Z_p²`, detecting both connected components. At `O_C/p`, weight zero is again `Z_p` in degree zero; the larger crystalline eigenspaces in §6.33 concern positive weights.

**Proof route.** `N = 0`: §6.25 and `R lim` give `Z_p(0)(R) = fib(φ − 1 on A_inf(R)) ≃ RΓ_ét(Spec R, Z_p)`; Gabber's affine analogue of proper base change passes to `R/p` and then to its perfection.

### 6.27 Picard groups of perfectoid rings are uniquely p-divisible

For a perfectoid ring `R` the groups `Pic(R)` and `Pic(R[1/p])` are uniquely p-divisible. Moreover the canonical map `Pic(R)^∧[−2] → Pic(R[1/p])^∧[−2]` of p-completed Picard groupoids is an isomorphism, compatible with `Pic(R)^∧[−2] ≃ Z_p(1)(R)` (§6.24), `Z_p(1)(R) ≃ RΓ_ét(Spec R[1/p], Z_p(1))` (§6.26) and the Kummer map `Pic(R[1/p])^∧[−2] → RΓ_ét(Spec R[1/p], Z_p(1))`.

`R` is any perfectoid ring. `Pic(R) = (τ^{≤1}RΓ(Spec R, G_m))[1]` denotes the Picard groupoid as an object of `D(Z)`; `Pic(R)` with roman type is its `H^0`, the Picard group. Cohomology of `G_m` is computed in the flat topology (equivalently étale).

Suggested.lean states the two Picard-group assertions as `picard_unique_p_divisibility`, with unique p-divisibility expressed by bijectivity of the p-th-power map on Mathlib `CommRing.Pic`.

(BS22, Corollary 9.7 and its proof; p. 76.)

*Needs:* §6.24, §6.26, PerfectoidSpaces P1.16–P1.18, P1.24–P1.25, SchemeAndStackFoundations §§2.1–2.6, Mathlib `CommRing.Pic`.

**Proof route.** `Pic(R) ≅ Pic(A_inf(R)) ≅ Pic(R^♭)` by deformation theory along the (`p`, d)-adically complete ring `A_inf(R)`, and the Picard group of a perfect ring is uniquely p-divisible (Frobenius is an automorphism inducing multiplication by `p`).

### 6.28 Z_p(n) is discrete and p-torsion-free

For each `n ≥ 0` the sheaf of complexes `Z_p(n)` on the quasisyntomic site is discrete and p-torsion-free: for every quasisyntomic ring `S`, `H^0(Z_p(n)(−))` is a sheaf of p-torsion-free abelian groups, and every class in `H^1(Z_p(n)(S))` vanishes on some quasisyntomic cover `S →` S' (so the quasisyntomic sheafification of `H^1(Z_p(n)(−))` is 0, and `H^i(Z_p(n)(−)) = 0` locally for `i ≠ 0`). Hence `Z_p(n)(S) = RΓ(S_qsyn, H^0(Z_p(n)))` is the cohomology of a sheaf of p-torsion-free groups. Two lemmas of the proof: (Lemma 14.4) for a perfectoid `Z_p[ζ_{p^∞}]^∧`-algebra `R`, R' `= R⟨X_1^{1/p^∞}`, …, `X_r^{1/p^∞}⟩` and `S = R'/(X_1`, …, `X_r`), the map `Z_p(n)(R') → Z_p(n)(S)` is surjective on `H^1`; (Lemma 14.5) for a perfectoid ring `R` and a surjection S' `→ S` of quasiregular semiperfectoid R-algebras such that the p-completion of `L_{S'/R} → L_{S/R}` is surjective on `H^{−1}`, the map `Δ_{S'} → Δ_S` is surjective.

The statement is local: discreteness means vanishing of `H^1` after quasisyntomic sheafification; `Z_p(n)(S)` itself can have `H^1 ≠ 0` (already `Z_p(0)(F_p)`). `n ≥ 0`; for `n < 0` the sheaf is 0. This is Conjecture 7.18 of BMS2. Its K-theoretic consequences, Corollaries 14.2 and 14.3 of Bhatt–Scholze (evenness of `K(−`; `Z_p`) locally and for regular quotients), need `K(−`; `Z_p) ≃ τ_{≥0}TC(−`; `Z_p`) on henselian pairs and are outside the scope of these syntomic targets. In the printed proof 'C/Q' reads `'C/Q_p` complete and algebraically closed'.

(BS22, Theorem 14.1, p. 97, and its proof, p. 100; BS22, Lemma 14.4 and its proof; p. 97; BS22, Lemma 14.5; p. 100; BMS2, Conjecture 7.18 and Remark 7.20; p. 51.)

*Needs:* §6.8, §6.11, §6.26, §6.27, §6.24, PrismaticCohomologyPartII §3.1, PrismaticCohomologyPartII §3.20, PerfectoidSpaces §P4.15.3, PrismaticCohomology §2.3, PrismaticCohomology §2.17, PrismaticCohomology §0.77, PerfectoidSpaces §P4.15.2, PerfectoidSpaces P4.15.4, PerfectoidSpaces P1.16–P1.18, P1.24–P1.25, DerivedModuleAlgebra §§0.1–0.17, 0.25, DerivedDeRhamCohomology §§5.1–5.7, PrismaticCohomologyPartII §3.2, PrismaticCohomologyPartII §3.3, PrismaticCohomologyPartII §3.25.

**Checks.**

- For `n=0` and `S=F_p`, global `H¹=Z_p` vanishes on the quasisyntomic cover by an algebraic closure; the local assertion does not erase global cohomology.
- For `n=1`, completed-unit classes vanish after a cover adjoining compatible roots; for `S=O_C` the group of such global `H¹` classes is already zero.
- For `p=5`, let `k=F̄_5` and `R=Γ(E∖{O},O_E)` for `E:y²=x³+x`. Here `#E(F_5)=4`, so `E` is ordinary, `R×=k×` has zero 5-adic completion, and `Pic(R)=E(k)` has `T_5Pic(R)=Z_5`. Kummer theory gives `H¹(Z_5(1)(R))=Z_5`. These classes require trivializing compatible torsion line bundles as well as adjoining roots. (The weight-one identification of §6.24; Stacks [Tag 040N](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/040N).)

**Proof route.** P-torsion-freeness of `H^0`: for `S` quasiregular semiperfectoid, `Δ_S` is a d-torsion-free δ-ring; if `x ∈ H^0` has px `= 0` then `φ(x) = 0`, so `d^n x = d^n φ_n(x) = φ(x) = 0` and `x = 0` (BMS2 Remark 7.20; by §3.20 `(b)` a class of `H^0` is the image of an element of `Fil^n_N Δ_S`, so the argument takes place in the δ-ring `Δ_S`).

### 6.29 The étale comparison morphism for syntomic cohomology

(1) For p-complete animated commutative ring `R` there is a canonical map of graded commutative ring objects of `D(Z)`, `⊕_n γ{n}`: `⊕_{n∈Z} RΓ_syn(Spf R, Z_p(n)) → ⊕_{n∈Z} RΓ_ét(Spec(R[1/p]), Z_p(n))`, characterised up to homotopy by functoriality in `R` and by `γ{1} ∘ c_1^syn ≃ c_1^ét`, where `c_1^ét`: `RΓ_ét(Spec R, G_m)[−1] → RΓ_ét(Spec R[1/p], Z_p(1))` is the étale first Chern class. (2) `γ{0}` is the composite of `α_R^{−1}`: `RΓ_syn(Spf R, Z_p) ≃ RΓ_ét(Spec R, Z_p)` with restriction to `Spec R[1/p]`. (3) For a perfectoid Z_p^cyc-algebra `R` with `Ext^i_{Z_p^cyc}(F_p, R) = 0` for all `i` and `x ↦ x^p − x` surjective on `R/pR`, the map `H^0_ét(Spf R, Z_p)[log_Δ(ε)] → ⊕_n RΓ_syn(Spf R, Z_p(n))` is an isomorphism of graded rings; in particular each `RΓ_syn(Spf R, Z_p(n))` is concentrated in degree 0. This applies to `O_C` for `C` algebraically closed and spherically complete of mixed characteristic. (4) For a derived p-complete animated Z[ζ_(p^∞)]-algebra `R`, the class `ε` of the compatible roots of unity lies in `H^0_syn(Spf R,Z_p(1))`; inverting it in the graded syntomic algebra gives the graded generic étale algebra after p-completion. In weight zero this is the p-completed colimit along multiplication by `ε` of the nonnegative syntomic weights. The statement for arbitrary animated `R` is in Syntomic cohomology of schemes, after that object is constructed.

`R` is a p-complete animated ring in (1), (2); a perfectoid algebra over `Z_p^cyc`, the p-completion of `Z[q^{1/p^∞}]/([p]_q)`, in (3); an animated `Z[ζ_{p^∞}]`-algebra in (4). `γ{n}` is in general not an isomorphism, and no range of degrees in which it is one is asserted here: the isomorphism statements are (4) after inverting `ε`, §6.26 for perfectoid rings and §6.20 for smooth formal schemes over `O_C`. Bhatt–Lurie Section 8 proves no range of degrees in which `γ{n}` is an isomorphism. (4) is a reformulation of Bhatt–Scholze Theorem 9.1 over the perfectoid base `Z_p^cyc` and depends on it. In the Bhatt–Lurie text the remark after Theorem 8.1.9 cites the wrong theorem and Remark 8.5.4 misprints a target.

(BL22, Theorem 8.3.1, Lemma 8.3.2, Proposition 8.3.3, Example 8.3.4, Proposition 8.3.7 and their proofs; pp. 193, 194; BL22, Section 8, introduction (the summary of Theorem 8.5.1); Theorem 8.5.1, Proposition 8.5.3, Remark 8.5.4 and the proof of the theorem; pp. 202, 203; BM23, Section 1.1, the fibre square after Example 1.4; p. 2.)

*Needs:* §6.9, §6.24, §6.10, §6.4, §6.5, PrismaticCohomologyPartII §4.19, PrismaticCohomologyPartII §4.25, PrismaticCohomology §0.77, PerfectoidSpaces §P4.15.2, SchemeAndStackFoundations §§2.1–2.6, PerfectoidSpaces P1.16–P1.18, P1.24–P1.25.

**Proof route.** Let `𝒞` be the perfectoid rings admitting a map from `Z_p^cyc` satisfying the two conditions of (3); `𝒞` contains products of `O_{C_i}` with `C_i` algebraically closed and spherically complete, hence is a basis for the arc_p-topology, and `R ↦ RΓ_ét(Spec R[1/p], Z_p(n))` is right Kan extended from `𝒞` (BM-arc, Corollary 6.17, p.51).

### 6.30 Syntomic cohomology of schemes

For an animated commutative ring `R` with p-completion R̂ and an integer `n`, `RΓ_syn(Spec R, Z_p(n))` is the pullback in `D̂(Z_p)` of `RΓ_syn(Spf` R̂, `Z_p(n)) → RΓ_ét(Spec` R̂[1/p], `Z_p(n)`) ← `RΓ_ét(Spec R[1/p], Z_p(n))`, the first map being `γ{n}` of §6.29; the projection to `RΓ_ét(Spec R[1/p], Z_p(n))` is again called the étale comparison morphism `γ{n}`. Properties: (a) if `R` is p-complete then `RΓ_syn(Spec R, Z_p(n)) = RΓ_syn(Spf R, Z_p(n))`; `(b)` if `p` is invertible in `R` then `γ{n}`: `RΓ_syn(Spec R, Z_p(n)) ≃ RΓ_ét(Spec R, Z_p(n))`; `(c)` with `j`: `Spec R[1/p] → Spec R` there is a fibre sequence `RΓ_ét(Spec R, j_!Z_p(n)) → RΓ_syn(Spec R, Z_p(n)) → RΓ_syn(Spf R, Z_p(n))`; `(d) R ↦ RΓ_syn(Spec R, Z_p(n))` satisfies fpqc descent, hence extends to schemes, formal schemes and algebraic stacks `X` by `RΓ_syn(X, Z_p(n)) = lim` over `Spec R → X`; `(e)` it is left Kan extended from smooth Z-algebras and commutes with filtered colimits; it does not commute with sifted colimits; `(f) RΓ_syn(Spec R, Z_p(0)) ≃ RΓ_ét(Spec R, Z_p)` canonically; `(g) c_1^syn`: `RΓ_ét(Spec R, G_m)[−1] → RΓ_syn(Spec R, Z_p(1))` exhibits the target as the p-completion of the source. For a scheme `X`, `Z/p^k(n)_X ∈ D(X_ét, Z/p^k)` denotes the restriction of `RΓ_syn(−, Z_p(n))/p^k` to the small étale site. `(h)` For animated Z[ζ_(p^∞)]-algebra `R`, inverting the compatible-root class `ε` in `⊕_nRΓ_syn(Spec R,Z_p(n))` and p-completing gives `⊕_nRΓ_ét(Spec R[1/p],Z_p(n))`, naturally as graded algebras (BL Theorem 8.5.1).

`R` is any animated commutative ring; the étale site and étale cohomology of `Spec R[1/p]` depend only on `π_0(R)`. The pullback is formed in the p-complete derived ∞-category; all four corners are p-complete. Étale cohomology of `Spec R[1/p]` with twisted coefficients, `j_!`, Gabber's affine proper base change and formal glueing for étale cohomology are imported (SchemeAndStackFoundations Layer 2 and the arc-descent requirement). In the printed proof of Proposition 8.4.10 the last colimit is indexed by ℰ where ℰ' is meant.

(BL22, Construction 8.4.1, Remarks 8.4.2 and 8.4.4, Proposition 8.4.6, Variant 8.4.7; pp. 196, 197, 198; BL22, Proposition 8.4.10, Corollary 8.4.11, Warning 8.4.12, Propositions 8.4.13 and 8.4.14; pp. 199, 200, 201; BM23, Notation 1.1; p. 1.)

*Needs:* §6.29, §6.9, §6.24, SchemeAndStackFoundations §§2.1–2.6, EnhancedDerivedSheaves E3, EnhancedDerivedSheaves §E5:animation.4.

- `Prismatic.Syntomic.SchemeDatum`: The imported cospan of an animated ring in weight `n`: `RΓ_syn(Spf` R̂, `Z_p(n)`), `RΓ_ét(Spec` R̂[1/p], `Z_p(n)`), `RΓ_ét(Spec R[1/p], Z_p(n))` with `γ{n}` and restriction (owners: this layer and SchemeAndStackFoundations Layer 2).
- `Prismatic.Syntomic.syntomicCohomologyScheme`: `RΓ_syn(Spec R, Z_p(n))`, the pullback of the cospan.
- `Prismatic.Syntomic.etaleComparison`: `γ{n}`: `RΓ_syn(Spec R, Z_p(n)) → RΓ_ét(Spec R[1/p], Z_p(n))`.
- `Prismatic.Syntomic.toFormal`: The map `RΓ_syn(Spec R, Z_p(n)) → RΓ_syn(Spf` R̂, `Z_p(n)`).
- `Prismatic.Syntomic.toFormal_isIso_of_complete`: If the restriction map of the cospan is an isomorphism (`R` p-complete), the map to the formal syntomic complex is an isomorphism.
- `Prismatic.Syntomic.etaleComparison_isIso_of_invertible`: If the two p-adic corners vanish (`p` invertible in `R`), `γ{n}` is an isomorphism.
- `Prismatic.Syntomic.pullback_condition`: The square commutes: `γ{n}` on Spf R̂ after the map to the formal complex equals restriction after `γ{n}`.

**Checks.**

- For a cospan with zero p-adic corners, `RΓ_syn(Spec R, Z_p(n)) ≅ RΓ_ét(Spec R[1/p], Z_p(n))`.
- For a cospan whose restriction map is the identity, `RΓ_syn(Spec R, Z_p(n)) ≅ RΓ_syn(Spf R, Z_p(n))`.
- For the zero cospan (the zero ring) `RΓ_syn = 0`.
- `RΓ_syn(Spec R, Z_p(n))` is not the direct sum of the formal and the étale corner: for the identity cospan with all three objects `M=F_p` in degree zero, the pullback is `F_p`, whereas the direct sum of the outer corners has dimension two. Nonzero infinite-dimensional objects can be isomorphic to their direct sum with themselves.

**Proof route.** Definition and (a), `(b)`: BL Construction 8.4.1 and Remark 8.4.2; for (a) the right vertical map of the square is an isomorphism, for `(b)` both lower corners vanish.

### 6.31 Connectivity, rigidity and left Kan extension of syntomic complexes

Let `i ≥ 0`. (1) The functor `R ↦ RΓ_syn(Spf R, Z_p(i))` on animated rings commutes with sifted colimits and is left Kan extended from finitely generated polynomial Z-algebras; on quasisyntomic rings `Z_p(i)` is left Kan extended from finitely generated p-complete polynomial Z_p-algebras. (2) For quasisyntomic ring `R`, `Z_p(i)(R) ∈ D^{≤ i+1}(Z_p)`. (3) (Rigidity) For a henselian pair `(R, I)` with `R` and `R/I` p-complete, `fib(Z_p(i)(R) → Z_p(i)(R/I)) ∈ D^{≤ i}(Z_p)`. (4) For a p-complete ring `R`, `H^{i+1}(F_p(i)(R)) ≅ coker(1 − C^{−1} : Ω^i_{R/p} → Ω^i_{R/p}/dΩ^{i−1}_{R/p})`; it vanishes when `R` is w-strictly local, in particular strictly henselian local. (5) Consequently, for every scheme `X` the étale sheaf of complexes `Z/p^n(i)_X` obtained by restricting syntomic cohomology (§6.30) to the small étale site lies in `D^{[0`, `i]}(X_ét, Z/p^n)`, and `Z/p^n(i)(R) ∈ D^{≤ i}` for `R` strictly henselian local.

Statements (2)–(4) are Theorems 5.1, 5.2 and Corollary 5.43 of Antieau–Mathew–Morrow–Nikolaus, as used by Bhatt–Mathew. In (3) both `R` and `R/I` are p-complete and `(R, I)` is henselian. The proofs in the cited sources of (2)–(4) describe Nygaard graded pieces as `gr^n THH` and use THH of graded rings; the direct prismatic argument requires the Nygaard graded continuity and internal-weight calculation specified above. (5) uses that the generic-fibre part of `Z/p^n(i)_X` is `μ_{p^n}^{⊗i}` in degree 0 and that stalks at characteristic-p points are computed on strictly henselian local rings by §6.12.

(BM23, Section 1.2, before Theorem 1.8; p. 3; BM23, Section 5.3, after Proposition 5.5; p. 24; AMMN22, Theorem 5.1, Theorem 5.2, Lemma 5.34, Proposition 5.35, Corollary 5.43; pp. 25, 35, 39; BL22, Proposition 5.5.19 and Remark 5.5.9; pp. 135, 137.)

*Needs:* §6.9, §6.8, §6.16, §6.12, §6.30, PrismaticCohomologyPartII §4.25, PrismaticCohomologyPartII §3.5, EnhancedDerivedSheaves E3, DerivedModuleAlgebra §§0.1–0.17, 0.25, SchemeAndStackFoundations §§2.1–2.6, CrystallineCohomology §§CR.4.1–CR.4.8, CR.DDR.4.5, CR.DDR.5.18–CR.DDR.5.22, DerivedDeRhamCohomology §§5.1–5.7.

Local target `Prismatic.Nygaard.graded_adicContinuity`: if `T` is noetherian, p-complete and J-complete, `T/p` is F-finite and `J` is finitely generated, then `gr_N^rΔ_T{i}≃lim_m gr_N^rΔ_(T/J^m){i}`, compatibly with Sen operators and twists. **Proof route.** Apply the finite-conjugate-stage fibre sequence with `Θ+r`; fixed truncations are pseudocoherent, so Artin–Rees for relative cotangent groups and formal functions give their continuity. Reconstruct the Nygaard-completed tower and use invariance of syntomic fibres under that completion (BL22 Remark 5.5.8, p.135, for the finite-conjugate-stage description; AMMN22 Proposition 5.36 and its proof, p.36, for adic continuity of the Nygaard graded pieces).

`Prismatic.Nygaard.internalWeight` equips each finite quotient `Δ̂_T{i}/N^{≥n}` and `N^{≥i}/N^{≥i+r}` with the internal nonnegative grading of a nonnegatively graded ring `T`: modulo `p`, can preserves weights and `φ_i` multiplies them by `p`. This assertion concerns finite quotients, since underlying direct sums need not commute with graded inverse limits. Construct the fractional-weight coaction on homogeneous QRSP root covers after adjoining `t^(1/p∞)`; cotangent exterior powers and their finite extensions recover integral weights when `T` has integral weights, and `F(t^w)=t^(pw)` proves the Frobenius rule (BM23 Remark 3.8, Construction 3.9, Remark 3.10, pp.10–11; AMMN22 Construction 5.40, Proposition 5.41, pp.37–38).

**Proof route.** For a split square-zero `T=A⊕N` with A completed finite polynomial and `N` finite, grade A in weight zero and `N` in weight one; each fixed cohomological degree of a finite Nygaard quotient has finite weight support, so weight-multiplying Frobenius and the product-filtration connectivity lemma bound the fibre of `can−φ_i` in `D≤i`. Polynomial resolution and sifted colimits reduce general nilpotent extensions to this case. Adic continuity and Néron–Popescu approximation, using sections to obtain a retract of the complete-case fibre, give henselian rigidity (AMMN Lemma 5.42, p.39; proof of Theorem 5.2, pp.36–37).

**Checks for the prerequisites.** On a ring concentrated in weight zero every grading is concentrated there. For `A⊕N` with `N≠0` in weight one, Frobenius sends that weight to `p` rather than preserving it; replacing the weight by a constant grading fails this test. The unit has weight zero and is never killed by the positive-weight pro-nilpotence argument. For `T=F_p[[x]]`, `J=(x)`, `gr_N^0` and its finite conjugate constituents are recovered from `T/(x^m)`; using only the individual quotient T/(x) would discard its x-adic coefficients.

**Proof route.** (1): BL Proposition 7.4.8 (§6.9 `(d)`); AMMN Theorem 5.1(2). (2): the cofibre of `N^{≥i+1}Δ_R{i} → N^{≥i}Δ_R{i}` is `N^iΔ_R{i} ∈ D^{≤ i}` (BL Proposition 5.5.19, from the fibre sequence for Nygaard graded pieces of Remark 5.5.8; §4.25); modulo `p`, can and `φ_i` on `N^{≥i+1}Δ_R{i}/p` respect the I-adic filtrations and `φ_i` is zero on graded pieces, so by the connectivity lemma `fib(can − φ_i) ∈ D^{≤` i+1} (AMMN Lemma 5.34, Proposition 5.35), first for p-complete polynomial algebras and then by (1).

### 6.32 Syntomic cohomology is not étale cohomology of the generic fibre in all degrees

The étale comparison morphism `γ{n}`: `RΓ_syn(Spf R, Z_p(n)) → RΓ_ét(Spec R[1/p], Z_p(n))` is not an isomorphism in general, and the `φ = 1` statement of §6.4 does not extend to the divided-Frobenius fibres `Z_p(n)`. Instances: (1) `R = Z_p`, `n = 1`: `H^1` of the source is the p-completion of `Z_p^×` and `H^1` of the target the p-completion of `Q_p^×`; `γ{1}` is injective on `H^1` with cokernel `Z_p`, generated by the Kummer class of `p`. (2) `R` an F_p-algebra: the target is 0 for every `n`, while `RΓ_syn(Spf R, Z_p) = RΓ_ét(Spec R, Z_p) ≠ 0` for `R ≠ 0`, and for `R = O_C/p` the group `H^0(Z_p(n)(R)) = A_crys^{φ=p^n}` is non-zero for all `n ≥ 0`. (3) For `𝔛` smooth over `Spf O_C` the sheaf `Z/p^k(n)` on `𝔛_ét` is the truncation `τ^{≤n}Rψ_*Z/p^k(n)`, so `Z/p^k(n) → Rψ_*Z/p^k(n)` is an isomorphism if and only if `R^jψ_*Z/p^k = 0` for all `j > n`. (4) For a p-torsion-free F-smooth scheme `X` (for example regular and flat over `Z`) with `j`: `X[1/p] → X`, Bhatt–Mathew prove that `Z/p^k(n)_X → τ^{≤n}Rj_*μ_{p^k}^{⊗n}` is an isomorphism on cohomology sheaves in degrees `< n` and injective in degree `n` with image generated by symbols from `O_X^×`; instance (1) is the case `X = Spec Z_p`, `n = 1`. For a p-complete `Z[ζ_{p^∞}]`-algebra `R` with its specified compatible roots of unity, there is an isomorphism after inverting the resulting class `ε` and p-completing (§6.29 (4)) and, for the fixed points of `φ` itself, §6.4.

The warning concerns the syntomic complexes `Z_p(n)`, built from the Nygaard filtration and the divided Frobenius; it does not concern the `φ = 1` statement of Theorem 9.1, which holds for every p-adic formal scheme over a perfectoid ring. (4) is quoted from Bhatt–Mathew Theorem 1.8 as the general shape of the discrepancy; its proof (F-smoothness, Bloch–Kato) is outside this layer and is not a prerequisite of any construction. In (1) `p` is odd for the explicit description `1 + pZ_p` of the completed units; the statement about the cokernel holds for all `p`.

(BM23, Theorem 1.8 and the paragraph after it; p. 3; BL22, Section 8, introduction; pp.188–189; BMS2, Theorem 10.1; p. 75.)

*Needs:* §6.29, §6.24, §6.15, §6.20, §6.4, SchemeAndStackFoundations §§2.1–2.6.

**Proof route.** (1): by §6.24 (3), `RΓ_syn(Spf Z_p, Z_p(1)) = RΓ_ét(Spec Z_p, G_m)^∧[−1] = (Z_p^×)^∧[−1]`, and `RΓ_ét(Spec Q_p, Z_p(1))` has `H^1 = lim Q_p^×/(Q_p^×)^{p^k}` by Kummer theory; `γ{1}` is induced by `Z_p^× ⊂ Q_p^×` by the characterisation of §6.29, and `Q_p^×/Z_p^× = Z`.

### 6.33 O_C, the formal torus, finite levels and the maps n → n − 1

Let `C` be a complete algebraically closed extension of `Q_p`, `A_inf = A_inf(O_C)`, `q = [ε^{1/p}]` with `ε` a compatible system of primitive p-power roots of unity, `d = [p]_q` a generator of `ker(A_inf → O_C)` and `μ = q − 1`, so `φ(μ) = dμ`. (1) For `n ≥ 0`, `Z_p(n)(O_C)` is concentrated in degree 0 and equals `Z_p` · `μ^n ⊂ φ^{−1}(d)^n A_inf`, the kernel of `φ/d^n − 1`; it is identified with `H^0(Spec C, Z_p(n)) = T_p(C^×)^{⊗n}`. (2) At finite level `Z/p^k(n)(O_C) = Z/p^k` · `μ^n = μ_{p^k}(C)^{⊗n}`, and the maps induced by `k → k − 1` are the reduction maps, compatible with those of étale cohomology; likewise in §6.4 the isomorphisms `Z/p^k ≃ (A_inf[1/d]/p^k)^{φ=1} = W_k(C^♭)^{F=1}` commute with reduction. (3) For the formal torus `𝔛 = Spf O_C⟨T^{±1}⟩` with `S = O_C⟨T^{±1}⟩`: `RΓ_ét(Spec S[1/p], Z/p^k) ≃ (Δ_{S/A_inf}[1/d]/p^k)^{φ=1}`, and on `𝔛_ét`, `Z/p^k(0) = Z/p^k` and `Z/p^k(1) ≃ τ^{≤1}Rψ_*μ_{p^k}`, whose `H^1` contains the Kummer class of `T`, a non-zero section of `R^1ψ_*μ_{p^k}`. (4) For `C` spherically complete, `⊕_n RΓ_syn(Spf O_C, Z_p(n)) = Z_p[log_Δ(ε)]`.

`C` is complete and algebraically closed; in (4) also spherically complete. The normalisation `q = [ε^{1/p}]` makes `[p]_q` a generator of the kernel of `θ`, as in the proof of Bhatt–Scholze Theorem 9.4. These are instances of earlier targets, collected as the layer’s worked examples; nothing new is proved.

(BS22, Proof of Theorem 9.4, first step; p. 73; BL22, Proposition 8.3.3 and Example 8.3.4; p. 194.)

*Needs:* §6.8, §6.26, §6.4, §6.5, §6.20, §6.29, PerfectoidSpaces P1.16–P1.18, P1.24–P1.25.

**Proof route.** (1): `Z_p(n)(O_C) = fib(φ/d^n − 1 : φ^{−1}(d)^n A_inf → A_inf)` (§6.8); `μ^n` lies in the kernel because `φ(μ^n) = d^nμ^n`; by §6.26 the complex is `RΓ(Spec C, Z_p(n))`, free of rank one in degree 0, and multiplication by `μ^{−n}` is the comparison used in that proof.

### Examples

For a perfectoid ring, Artin–Schreier–Witt fixed points recover constant `Z/p^n` coefficients; localization at `t=0` gives the zero ring and zero complex. For a perfectoid ring `R`, weight-zero syntomic cohomology is `RΓ_ét(Spec R,Z_p)`; it is `Z_p` in degree zero for `R=O_C` with `C` algebraically closed. At `R=F_p` its `H⁰` and `H¹` are both `Z_p`, and at `R=O_C×O_C` its `H⁰` is `Z_p²`. Negative weights vanish, and weight one is completed `G_m` cohomology shifted by `[−1]`. For a characteristic-p scheme the p-inverted generic fibre is empty, but weight-zero syntomic cohomology is nonzero on a connected scheme. Thus syntomic cohomology cannot be all-degree generic-fibre étale cohomology. The nearby-cycle assertion uses its degree truncation and the correct twist.

**§6.2.** For `B = F_p[t]` and the completed direct sum of copies of `F_p[[t]]` indexed by a set `J`: the fixed points are the direct sum over `J` of `F_p ⊕ F_p[−1]`. For a derived p-complete simplicial algebra `S` over a perfectoid ring: `(Δ_{S/A}[1/d]/p)^{φ=1} ≃ (Δ_{S/A,perf}[1/d]/p)^{φ=1}`, the step used in Theorem 9.1. Without derived t-completeness of the terms the statement `N^{φ=1} ≃ (N/t)^{φ=1}` fails: `N = B[1/t]` has `N/t = 0`.

**§6.3.** `S = O_C`, `C` complete algebraically closed: both sides are `Z/p^n` in degree `0 (W_n(C^♭)^{F=1} = Z/p^n` and `F − 1` is surjective). `S` a perfect F_p-algebra: both sides are 0. `S = Z_p^cycl` (the p-completion of `Z_p[ζ_{p^∞}]`): `H^1` of the right-hand side is `coker(F − 1)` on `W_n` of the tilt of the cyclotomic field, the group `Hom_cont(Gal, Z/p^n)`.

**§6.4.** `X = Spf O_C`: both sides are `Z/p^n`. `X = Spf O_C⟨T^{±1}⟩` (formal torus): after a choice of `ε`, `H^1` of both sides is the group of units of `C⟨T^{±1}⟩` modulo `p^n-th` powers (Kummer theory; `C⟨T^{±1}⟩` is a principal ideal domain); it contains the class of `T`, of order `p^n`, and is not finitely generated. For the wild unit classes choose coefficients `a_j` with distinct valuations `0<v(a_j)<v(p)`. For a nonempty finite product `f=∏(1+a_jT)^{e_j}`, with `e_j∈{0,…,p−1}`, the logarithmic derivative `f′/f=∑e_j a_j/(1+a_jT)` has Gauss norm `|a_j|>|p|` for the unique nonzero term of least valuation. If `f=g^p` for an analytic unit `g`, then `‖g‖=1` and its reduction satisfies `ḡ^p=1`, hence `ḡ=1`. Consequently `‖g′/g‖<1`, while `f′/f=p g′/g` would have norm strictly less than |p|, a contradiction. Thus these Kummer classes are F_p-linearly independent at level `p`. By comparison, units 1+aT with `v(a)>p·v(p)/(p−1)` have a convergent binomial p-th root. A singular input, `S = O_C⟨x`, y⟩/(xy): the statement holds with the derived `Δ_{S/A}`; no smoothness is used. `X` of characteristic `p`: both sides vanish. For `S` perfectoid the map is the Artin–Schreier–Witt/tilting isomorphism.

**§6.5.** `X = Spf O_C`: `Z_p ≃ (W(C^♭))^{φ=1}`, and the square for `n → n − 1` is reduction `Z/p^n → Z/p^{n−1}`. Finite level: for the formal torus the Kummer class of `T` at level `n` maps to the Kummer class of `T` at level `n − 1` on both sides. For `S = Z_p^cycl` the `lim^1` term vanishes and `H^1 = Hom_cont(Gal, Z_p)`.

**§6.6.** `X = Spf O_C⟨T^{±1}⟩` over `(A_inf, ker θ)`: `H^1_ét(X_η, μ_{p^n})` is the group of units of `C⟨T^{±1}⟩` modulo `p^n-th` powers, and the class of `T` corresponds to a φ-fixed class in `H^1` of `RΓ_Δ(X/A_inf)/p^n[1/d]`. `X` proper smooth over `O_C`: with §1.23, `RΓ_ét(X_C, Z/p^n)` is the fixed points of `φ` on a perfect complex over `A_inf/p^n[1/d] = W_n(C^♭)`. The statement for singular `X` is §6.4, not this target.

**§6.7.** `R = O_C`: `X = Spec C` has cohomological dimension `0 ≤ 1`. `R = Z_p^cycl`: `X` is the spectrum of the completed cyclotomic field, whose absolute Galois group has p-cohomological dimension `≤ 1`; `H^1_ét(X, F_p) = Hom_cont(Gal, F_p)` is non-zero, so the bound 1 is attained. `R` a perfect F_p-algebra: `X` is empty and the statement is vacuous.

**§6.9.** For `R` quasiregular semiperfectoid the complex is the two-term complex of §6.8. For `R` a quasiregular semiperfect F_p-algebra it is `Fil^n_N A_crys(R) → A_crys(R)`, `x ↦ φ(x)/p^n − x` (BL Example 7.4.4). `RΓ_syn(Spf Z[1/p], Z_p(n)) = 0`, since the p-completion of `Z[1/p]` is 0. For `𝔛 = Spf R` the limit definition returns `RΓ_syn(Spf R, Z_p(n))`.

**§6.11.** `i = 0`: every `m ≥ 1` works when `p ≥ 2`, and `φ` maps `N^{≥m}/p` into `N^{≥pm}/p`. `i = 1`, `p = 2`: `m ≥ 3`; `i = 1`, `p` odd: `m ≥ 2`. `A = O_C`, `i = 1`: `N^{≥m}A_inf/p = ξ^m O_C^♭` and `φ_1(ξ^m x) = ξ^{p(m−1)} x^p`, which lies in `ξ^{m+1}O_C^♭` exactly when `p(m − 1) ≥ m + 1`.

**§6.12.** `O_C = colim` of the rings of integers of finite extensions, p-completed: `Z/p^r(i)(O_C)` is the colimit of the complexes of the p-completed finite levels. A filtered colimit of perfectoid rings: `Z/p^r(i)` of the p-completed colimit is the colimit, as used in the proof of Bhatt–Scholze Theorem 9.4. The statement fails for `Z_p(i)` without reduction: `Z_p(0)` of a filtered colimit is p-complete, the colimit of `Z_p(0)` need not be.

**§6.13.** `i = 0`: `0 → Z_p → W(O_X) → W(O_X) → 0` with `F − 1`, the Artin–Schreier–Witt sequence on the pro-étale site. `i = 1`, `X = G_m`: `dlog[T] ∈ WΩ^1` is fixed by `F`, since `F(dlog[T]) = dlog[T]`. `X = Spec k`, `i ≥ 1`: `N^{≥i}W(k) = p^{i−1}VW(k) = p^iW(k)` and `(φ_i − 1)(p^i x) = F(x) − p^i x` is bijective onto `W(k)`, so `WΩ^i_{k,log} = 0` and the cokernel vanishes.

**§6.14.** `S` perfect, `i ≥ 1`: `A_crys(S) = W(S)`, `N^{≥i} = p^iW(S)`, `φ_i − 1 = (F − p^i)/p^i`, surjective because `F` is bijective and `p^iF^{−1}` is contracting. `S = F_p`, `i = 0`: `φ − 1 = 0` on `Z_p` is not surjective, but becomes surjective on `W` of an algebraic closure. `S = F_p[x^{1/p^∞}]/(x)`, `i = 1`: the class of the divided power `γ_p(x)` is hit, since it lies in `Fil^p_pd`.

**§6.15.** `S` perfect: `Z_p(i)(S) = W(S)^{F=p^i} = 0` for `i > 0`, and `Z_p(0)(S) = fib(F − 1 on W(S))`. `S = O_C/p`: `H^0(Z_p(i)(S)) = A_crys^{φ=p^i}` contains `t^i` for `t = log[ε]`, since `φ(t) =` pt; after inverting `p` these are the graded pieces of the ring of the Fargues–Fontaine curve. `S = F_p`, `i = 0`: `H^1 = Z_p ≠ 0`, the counterexample to the uncorrected statement. `i = 1`: `A_crys(S)^{φ=p} ≅ T_p(S^×)` via `x ↦ log[x]` (§6.24).

**§6.16.** `i = 0`: `Rλ_* Z_p(0) = Z_p`, the constant pro-étale sheaf `lim Z/p^r`. `i = 1`: `Rλ_* Z_p(1) = WΩ^1_log[−1]`, matching the classical fact that the pushforward of `μ_{p^n}` from the flat site is `W_nΩ^1_log[−1]`. `A = k`: `Z_p(i)(k) = 0` for `i ≥ 1`. `A = k[T^{±1}]`, `i = 1`: `dlog[T]` is a non-zero section of `WΩ^1_log`.

**§6.17.** `i = 0`: the complex is `fib(φ − 1 on Ã/p^n)` étale-locally, namely `Z/p^n` in degree 0. `𝔛 = Spf O_C`: `Z/p^n(i) = Z/p^n(i)(C)` in degree 0 for all `i`. `𝔛` the formal torus of dimension 1 and `i = 0`: the degree-1 term contributes nothing, since `ξ̃φ − 1` is invertible on Ã/p. The basis elements `d_q log T_{a_1} ∧ ⋯ ∧ d_q log T_{a_i}` of degree `i` are fixed by `φ_i`.

**§6.18.** `i = j = 0`: empty; `i = j = 1`: `1 − ξφ^{−1}` is bijective on `Ô_X^{♭+}/μ`. `X = Spa(C, O_C)` with `C` algebraically closed: `1 − ξ^iφ^{−1}` is surjective on `O_C^♭` with kernel `F_p` · `μ^i` modulo `p`. For a non-algebraically-closed `C` the assertion is about pro-étale sheaves, not surjectivity on global sections; at `i=0` the cyclotomic perfectoid field with residue field `F_p` gives a negative control, since F−1 cannot hit 1 in its valuation ring. The kernel `Z/p^n` · `μ^i` is identified with `Z/p^n(i)` by `ε^{⊗i} ↦ μ^i` for the chosen `ε` (over `C` every Tate twist is trivialised by `ε`); the identification is canonical only after twisting `A_inf` by `{i}`.

**§6.19.** `j = 0` in Lemma 10.8: `H^0(Lη_μ C) = H^0(C)` because `H^0(C)` is μ-torsion-free. `i = 0`: both truncated fibres are the kernel of `1 − φ^{−1}` on `H^0(C)`. For `C = Rν_*A_inf,X/p^n` the lemma compares fixed points on `τ^{≤i}AΩ/p^n` and on `Rν_*A_inf,X/p^n`.

**§6.20.** `𝔛 = Spf O_C`: `Z/p^n(i)(O_C) ≃ Z/p^n(i)(C)` in degree 0, agreeing with §6.26. `𝔛 = Spf O_C⟨T^{±1}⟩` and `i = 1`: `H^1` of both sides contains the Kummer class of `T`, which is non-zero in `R^1ψ_*μ_{p^n}`. `i = 0`: `Z/p^n ≃ τ^{≤0}Rψ_*Z/p^n = ψ_*Z/p^n`. Compatibility in `n`: the squares with `Z/p^n(i) → Z/p^{n−1}(i)` commute.

**§6.21.** For a perfect base prism, `A_∞=A` and `R_∞=R`, giving Theorem 9.1. For a crystalline base the base-changed `R_∞` has characteristic `p`, its generic fibre is empty, and localising `Δ` at `d_∞=p` before reducing modulo `p^n` gives zero. Changing `d_∞` by a unit has no effect because only localisation at its ideal occurs.

**§6.22.** For `R` perfect, `J=0` and `φ/p−1:pW(R)→W(R)` is bijective via multiplication by `p` followed by `1−pφ^(-1)`. The nontrivial kernel for a semiperfect quotient consists of lifted units 1+J, not all units of `R^♭`.

**§6.23.** For a perfect affine ring the chartwise kernel is zero. Using Pic alone in place of untruncated `RΓ_ét(G_m)` loses the Brauer contribution and fails on fields with p-primary Brauer classes.

**§6.24.** `Z_p(0)(O_C) = Z_p` in degree 0; `Z_p(0)(F_p) = RΓ_ét(Spec F_p, Z_p)` has `H^1 = Z_p`. `Z_p(1)(O_C) = T_p(O_C^×) = Z_p(1)` in degree 0, since `O_C^×` is p-divisible and `Pic(O_C) = 0`. `Z_p(1)(Z_p)` has `H^1 = (Z_p^×)^∧ = 1 + pZ_p` for `p` odd and `H^0 = T_p(Z_p^×) = 0`. At `p=2` the completed unit group is {±1} `× (1+4Z_2)`; the order-two class of −1 survives, so treating the logarithm on `1+2Z_2` as injective would lose torsion. `Z_p(−1)(R) = 0` for every `R`.

**§6.25.** `S = O_C`: `Z/p^n ≃ (A_inf/p^n)^{φ=1} = W_n(O_C^♭)^{F=1}`, and `F − 1` is surjective on `W_n(O_C^♭)`. `S = Z_p^cycl`: `H^1` of both sides is `H^1_ét(Spec F_p, Z/p^n) = Z/p^n`, since the residue field of `S` is `F_p`. `S` an F_p-algebra over a perfect ring `R`: `Δ_{S/A}/p` is derived de Rham cohomology up to Frobenius twist and the statement is the Artin–Schreier sequence.

**§6.26.** `R = O_C`: `Z_p(n)(O_C) ≃ Z_p(n)` in degree 0, a free Z_p-module of rank one, for every `n ≥ 0`. `R` perfect of characteristic `p` and `n ≥ 1`: `Z_p(n)(R) = 0`; for `n = 0`, `Z_p(0)(R) = RΓ_ét(Spec R, Z_p) = W(R)^{F=1}` derived. `R = Z_p^cycl`: `H^1(Z_p(n)(R)) = H^1(Gal` of the completed cyclotomic field, `Z_p(n)`), non-zero. Finite level: `Z/p^k(n)(O_C) = μ_{p^k}^{⊗n}(C)`, and the maps for `k → k − 1` are the reduction maps.

**§6.27.** `R = O_C`: `Pic(O_C) = 0` and `Pic(C) = 0`. `R` perfect of characteristic `p`: `R[1/p] = 0` and `Pic(R)` is uniquely p-divisible because Frobenius acts as an automorphism and as multiplication by `p`. Consequence used in Theorem 14.1: `H^1(Z_p(1)(R))` is `H^0` of the derived p-completion of `R^×`.

**§6.28.** `n = 0`: `Z_p(0)` is the constant sheaf `lim Z/p^r`, and `H^1(Z_p(0)(F_p)) = Z_p` dies on the cover `F_p →` algebraic closure. `n = 1`: `Z_p(1)=T_pG_m` as a quasisyntomic sheaf. Classes from completed units die after adjoining compatible p-power roots. General `H¹` classes can also contain compatible p-power-torsion line bundles: trivialize those bundles on a quasisyntomic refinement and then adjoin the required roots. The identification with completed units applies over perfectoid rings by §6.27, rather than over every quasisyntomic ring. `S = O_C⟨X^{1/p^∞}⟩/(X)`: `H^1(Z_p(n)(S))` is a quotient of `H^1(Z_p(n)(O_C⟨X^{1/p^∞}⟩))` by Lemma 14.4. In characteristic `p` the statement is §6.15.

**§6.29.** `R = O_C` with `C` algebraically closed and spherically complete: `⊕_n RΓ_syn(Spf O_C, Z_p(n)) = Z_p[log_Δ(ε)]` and `γ` sends `log_Δ(ε)` to the generator `ρ(ε)` of `Z_p(1)(C)`; `γ{n}` is an isomorphism for `n ≥ 0` and `0 → Z_p(n)(C)` for `n < 0`. `R = Z_p`, `n = 1`: `γ{1}` on `H^1` is the inclusion of the p-completion of `Z_p^×` into that of `Q_p^×`, with cokernel `Z_p`; not an isomorphism. `R` an F_p-algebra: the target vanishes, and (4) says that `ε` acts nilpotently up to p-completion: colim along `ε` of `RΓ_syn(Spf R, Z_p(n))` is 0. `n = 0`: `γ{0}` is restriction `RΓ_ét(Spec R, Z_p) → RΓ_ét(Spec R[1/p], Z_p)`.

**§6.31.** `i = 0`: `Z/p^n(0)_X = Z/p^n` in degree 0. `i = 1`: `Z/p^n(1)_X = fib(p^n : G_m → G_m)` on `X_ét`, in degrees [0, 1]. `R = Z_p`, `i = 1`: `Z_p(1)(Z_p) = (Z_p^×)^∧[−1] ∈ D^{≤ 2}`; the fibre to `Z_p(1)(F_p) = 0` is in `D^{≤ 1}`. `R` strictly henselian local: `H^{i+1}(F_p(i)(R)) = 0`.

**§6.32.** The cokernel of `γ{1}` on `H^1` for `R = Z_p` is `Z_p`, not 0. For `R = F_p`: `RΓ_syn(Spf F_p, Z_p)` has `H^0 = H^1 = Z_p` while `Spec F_p[1/p]` is empty. For `R = O_C`: `γ{n}` is an isomorphism for every `n ≥ 0`, so the discrepancy is invisible on the basis of the arc_p-topology. A statement `'Z_p(n)(R) ≃ RΓ_ét(Spec R[1/p], Z_p(n))` for every quasisyntomic R' is false by (1).

**§6.33.** `φ(μ) = dμ`, hence `φ(μ^n)/d^n = μ^n`. `Z/p^k(n)(O_C) → Z/p^{k−1}(n)(O_C)` is surjective with kernel `p^{k−1}Z/p^k` · `μ^n`. The Kummer class of `T` in `H^1_ét(Spec C⟨T^{±1}⟩, μ_{p^k})` has order `p^k`. `Z_p(n)(O_C) ≠ 0` for every `n ≥ 0`, whereas for `n>0`, `H⁰(Z_p(n)(O_C/p))=A_crys^{φ=p^n}` is much larger. In weight zero both `O_C` and `O_C/p` give `Z_p` in degree zero.

### Dependencies

Layers 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5; AInfCohomology Layer AI.1, AInfCohomology §§AI.1.1–AI.1.2, AInfCohomology §§AI.1.1–AI.1.2, AInfCohomology §AI.1.2, AInfCohomology §§AI.3.1–AI.3.3, AInfCohomology §§AI.4.1–AI.4.4, ClassicalAdicEtaleCohomology §§1.1–1.4, ClassicalAdicEtaleCohomology §§4.1–4.5, SchemeAndStackFoundations §0.14, §2.15; ClassicalAdicEtaleCohomology §2.1, DerivedDeRhamCohomology §§PD.6–PD.7; CrystallineCohomology §§CR.DDR.5.20–CR.DDR.5.21, DerivedDeRhamCohomology §§PD.4–PD.5, CrystallineCohomology §§CR.4.1–CR.4.8, CR.DDR.4.5, CR.DDR.5.18–CR.DDR.5.22, CrystallineCohomology §CR.4.1, CrystallineCohomology §§CR.4.1, CR.4.5, CrystallineCohomology §CR.4.2, DerivedModuleAlgebra §§0.1–0.17, 0.25, DerivedModuleAlgebra §§1.1–1.9; DerivedDeRhamCohomology §§1.10–1.23, DerivedDeRhamCohomology §4.2; CrystallineCohomology §§CR.DDR.4.1, CR.DDR.4.3–CR.DDR.4.4, CR.DDR.5.8–CR.DDR.5.10, DerivedDeRhamCohomology §§5.1–5.7, EnhancedDerivedSheaves E2, EnhancedDerivedSheaves E3, EnhancedDerivedSheaves §E5:abstract.4, EnhancedDerivedSheaves §§E5:animation.1 and E5:animation.4, PerfectoidSpaces P1.16–P1.18, P1.24–P1.25, PerfectoidSpaces P4.15.4, PerfectoidSpaces P4.15.5, PerfectoidSpaces P3, SchemeAndStackFoundations §§2.1–2.6. Exact inputs are given in the supplier contracts; internal construction dependencies are linked above.

## Layer 7: Prismatic F-crystals and crystalline lattices

Develop descent and realizations for finite locally free prismatic F-crystals and their Laurent analogues. The integral crystalline lattice equivalence uses analytic continuation, almost Galois descent and period-theoretic inputs; its mod-p analogue fails.

### 7.0 Graded prismatic square and filtered Frobenius bundle dictionary

Construct the absolute trace–prismatic comparison using the filtered cyclotomic theory of AInfCohomology AI.7.2: THH, TC⁻, TP, their actual cyclotomic Frobenius, the quasisyntomic trace sheaf and its `AΩ` and characteristic-p comparisons. For a perfectoid base `B`, `A=A_inf(B)`, `I=(d)`, let `D^tr_nc` be the left Kan extension of the smooth trace coefficient theory in derived (p,d)-complete A-complexes, carrying its extended Nygaard filtration. Its Nygaard completion is the unfolded coefficient complex `D̂^tr`; on QRSP `S` it is `π₀TC⁻(S;Z_p)≃π₀TP(S;Z_p)`. Construct natural equivalences `D^tr_nc(S)≃Δ_S` and `D̂^tr_S≃Δ̂_S` of filtered E∞-algebras, preserving twists, can, divided/cyclotomic Frobenius and the functorial δ-structure, including when `S` has p-torsion. The noncompleted object is not defined by `π₀` of global TC⁻ for arbitrary `S`.

Define `Prismatic.Trace.TC(R) = fib(can−φ: TC⁻(R;Z_p) → TP(R;Z_p))`. Unfold the filtered fibre from the double-speed Postnikov filtrations on QRSP rings. For quasisyntomic `R`, identify

```text
gr^n TC⁻(R;Z_p) ≃ N^{≥n}Δ̂_R{n}[2n],
gr^n TP(R;Z_p) ≃ Δ̂_R{n}[2n],
gr^n TC(R;Z_p) ≃ Z_p(n)(R)[2n].
```

The first two filtrations are imported from AI.7.2; the new maps to their prismatic coefficients identify can and divided Frobenius, with multiplicative, finite-coefficient and twist compatibility. To identify `can−φ` with §6.8's `φ−can` fibre, multiply its target by −1. Completion invariance is §6.9. The relative sphere-polynomial/Breuil–Kisin agreement is constructed below from AI.7.3's trace-defined complexes.

**Proof route.** Reduce by root covers and Künneth to the cyclotomic regular quotient, use §3.26's q-divided-power recognition map, and test this actual map by the supplied crystalline comparison modulo `(p,d)`. Derived Nakayama and quasisyntomic unfolding give the noncompleted equivalence; §3.25 extends `δ` continuously to its Nygaard completion, and the filtered fibre gives the three graded identifications. (BS22 Theorem 13.1, pp.94–96; BMS2 Construction 7.12 and Proposition 7.13, pp.48–49, Corollary 9.12, pp.73–74, Theorem 8.17, pp.62–64, Theorem 1.12(3)–(5), pp.6–7; BL22 Proposition 7.4.6, pp.178–179.)

The API is `Prismatic.Trace.noncompletedComparison`, `nygaardCompletedComparison`, `deltaCompatibility`, `gradedTCMinus`, `gradedTP`, `gradedTC` and `gradedTC_can_phi`, with naturality, twist, cup-product and mod-p^r compatibility.

**Checks.**

- On a perfectoid `B` the completed comparison is `A_inf(B)` with its coefficient Frobenius; in characteristic `p` it is `W(B)`. For `B=F_p`, weight-zero TC has `π₀=π_(−1)=Z_p`, as does the two-term syntomic complex with zero differential. Losing the fibre shift would remove the second group.
- In weight one over a perfectoid base, the source is `φ^(−1)(d)A`, and its map is `φ/d−can`. Replacing this by `φ−1` on A gives the wrong twist.
- For the key semiperfectoid quotient the q-divided-power basis maps to its prescribed trace basis; reduction to A/(p,d) is the crystalline comparison. Equality of two unnamed reductions is insufficient.
- For a smooth torus the unfolded coefficient object has differential-form cohomology. It is not obtained by taking the discrete `π₀` of its global TC⁻ spectrum.
- The zero ring gives zero throughout. Negative-weight graded TC vanishes by Layer 6’s low-weight syntomic theorem, while the coefficient TP theory still has negative graded pieces.

**Relative trace–prismatic Breuil–Kisin comparison.** Construct `Prismatic.Trace.relativeBreuilKisinComparison` using the mixed-characteristic DVR and Breuil–Kisin prism of Conventions. For a quasiregular semiperfectoid quasisyntomic O_K-algebra `S`, identify `π₀TP(S/𝕊[z];Z_p)` with `N̂(φ_𝔖^*Δ_(S/𝔖))` as δ-rings with Nygaard filtration. For a p-completely smooth O_K-algebra `R`, identify AI.7.3's `D_𝔖^tr(R)` with `Δ_(R/𝔖)` and its `D̂_tw(R)` with the Nygaard-completed Frobenius pullback. Require compatibility with can, Frobenius, twists, multiplicative structures and the supplied de Rham, crystalline and A_inf specialization maps; preserve the `𝔖^(−1)` scalar structure of the twisted trace object. (BS22 Proposition 15.7 and the reconstruction immediately following it, p.105.)

Consume AI.7.2's coefficient calculation with its fixed monic `E`: `bv=E`, `can(b)=Eσ`, `can(v)=σ⁻¹`, `φ(b)=ασ` and `φ(v)=φ(E)α⁻¹σ⁻¹`, with `α∈(𝔖^(−1))×`. Under `(b,v,σ)↦(cb,c⁻¹v,cσ)` the unit becomes `φ(c)α/c`. Transport these bases through the comparison: in the supplied `can⁻¹φ` convention the localized degree-two multiplier is `α/E`, and its tensor powers and duals have multipliers `(α/E)^n`, `n∈Z`. The divided-Frobenius and canonical Breuil–Kisin twist identifications must respect these factors and their semilinear basis changes, including under specialization.

**Proof route.** Use the absolute comparison above after the flat Breuil–Kisin to A_inf extension of AI.7.1, with Frobenius transport on the twisted coefficients. On QRSP objects this proves that cyclotomic Frobenius is a Frobenius lift. The prismatic universal property constructs the comparison map; check its Nygaard compatibility and equivalence after the same complete faithfully flat extension and descend. Unfold to smooth algebras, and recover the untwisted object by the high-Nygaard divided-Frobenius reconstruction of BS22 p.105. Carry the trace descent Frobenius, its coefficient unit and the specialization maps through that reconstruction; identify twists with the transported trace lines and their indicated multipliers.

*Needs:* The absolute trace comparison of this section; AInfCohomology §AI.7.1 (`CohomologicalBK.flatCoefficientExtension`), §AI.7.2 (relative traces and their specializations), §AI.7.3 (`CohomologicalBK.twistedTrace`, `CohomologicalBK.traceDescent`); PrismaticCohomologyPartII §§3.25–3.26, 5.13–5.16; DerivedModuleAlgebra §1.9 (complete faithfully flat descent).

**Checks.**

- At `R=O_K`, the untwisted object is `𝔖` and the twisted object is `𝔖^(−1)`; the cyclotomic coefficient map is `φ_𝔖`, not the identity. Under the shifted prism embedding its image of `u` is `[π^♭]^p`.
- On these relative coefficients, `can(bv)=E` and `φ(bv)=φ(E)`; the latter uses cancellation of `α` with `α⁻¹`. The localized multiplier is `α/E`, rather than `1/E` in an arbitrary trace basis.
- For `K=Q_p`, `π=p`, `E=u−p`, residue specialization `u↦0` gives `E↦−p` and `α(0)=−1`, since `can(b̄)=−pσ̄=pφ(b̄)`. Setting `α=1` would force `2pσ̄=0` in `Z_p[σ̄±1]`, impossible also at `p=2`. (NS18, Corollary IV.4.16, p.130, and the paragraph following it, p.131; BMS2 Lemma 11.6.) A compatible unit basis change leaves this constant term unchanged because Witt Frobenius on `Z_p` is the identity.
- For a smooth framed torus, the unfolded comparison has differential-form cohomology; replacing it by global `π₀TP` loses those groups. After A_inf extension its coefficient map is the absolute comparison with the indicated Frobenius pullback.
- The untwisted de Rham specialization is along the Frobenius-transported scalar map, while the twisted trace object's specialization is along `u↦π` on its untwisted copy of the coefficients; identifying these two scalar maps without transport fails already at `O_K`.

**Linear cyclic theory and the rational trace square.** Define `HH(R/Z;Z_p)` as the derived p-completed relative cyclic bar object over HZ, with its circle action; HC, HC⁻ and HP are its p-completed homotopy orbits, homotopy fixed points and Tate construction. The norm triangle is `ΣHC→HC⁻→HP`. Linearization is `THH(R)⊗_(THH(Z))HZ`, with the relative-bar map, rather than tensor over the sphere. Quasisyntomic unfolding gives complete multiplicative filtrations with `gr^nHC⁻≃Fil_H^n LΩ̂_R[2n]` and `gr^nHP≃LΩ̂_R[2n]`, where this hat includes Hodge and p-adic completion; for p-torsion-free QRSP `R` these are even theories.

For ordinary commutative `R`, construct the functorial pullback square

```text
TC(R;Q_p)   → TC(R/p;Q_p)
    ↓                  ↓
HC⁻(R;Q_p)  → HP(R;Q_p).
```

Here each `Q_p` theory is p-completion followed by inversion of `p`. The top relative fibre is `ΣHC(R;Q_p)`, its relative cofibre `Σ²HC(R;Q_p)`. In each fixed finite homotopy range require a natural relative comparison zigzag with kernels and cokernels killed by one power of `p` depending only on that range, uniformly in `R`.

**Proof route.** The cyclic-bar, bounded Postnikov and finite-weight filtrations give the quasi-isogeny comparison for the connective map `R⊗_𝕊F_p→R/p`, with bounds uniform in `R` in each finite range. Linearize the relative cyclotomic object to `HH(R/Z;Z_p)` with zero Frobenius, whose TC is the suspended circle-orbit object; the norm triangle supplies the square and both shifts. (BMS2 Theorem 1.17, p.9, Lemma 5.14, pp.34–35; AMMN22 Theorem 2.20, Lemmas 2.21–2.23, pp.12–14, Remark 2.25, p.14, Theorem 3.4 and Propositions 3.3–3.7, pp.15–17, Corollary 3.9 and Theorem 3.10, pp.18–19; use their cyclotomic argument, without introducing algebraic K-theory.)

The API is `Prismatic.Trace.HH`, `HC`, `HCMinus`, `HP`, `circleNormTriangle`, `linearization`, `cyclicHKRFiltration`, `cyclicGraded`, `Prismatic.Trace.rationalBeilinson` and `relativeComparison_boundedDenominators`. Circle constructions are the supplied AI.7.2 operations applied to this relative-bar object; these names do not introduce duplicate spectral constructions.

**Checks.**

- The cyclic bar of the base `Z` relative to itself is HZ. Its p-completed HC⁻ has one `Z_p` in every nonpositive even homotopy degree, HP one in every even degree, and HC one in every nonnegative even degree. The norm triangle has `ΣHC` on its left, so the cofibre of `HC⁻→HP` is `Σ²HC`.
- For `R=F_p` the top map of the rational square is the identity, so `HC⁻(F_p;Q_p)→HP(F_p;Q_p)` is an equivalence. The rational cyclic homotopy-orbit term vanishes; this does not assert that homotopy fixed points or Tate fixed points vanish. Inverting `p` before those infinite limits is invalid. `TC(F_p;Q_p)` itself is nonzero, so rationalizing the input ring before p-completed TC also gives the wrong theory.
- At the zero ring all four corners and the relative comparison vanish. At `p=2` the same rational statement holds; no low-degree integral isomorphism range is asserted.
- The coefficient in homotopy degree 2n of `Σ²HC` is `HC_(2n−2)`, not `HC_(2n)`. For `n=0` this is zero on a connective base.

**Prismatic Beilinson square.** For p-torsion-free quasisyntomic `R` and `n≥0`, construct `χ_n` and the natural cartesian square in `D(Q_p)`

```text
Q_p(n)(R)            → Q_p(n)(R/p)
   ↓                        ↓ χ_n
Fil_H^n LΩ_R[1/p]    → LΩ_R[1/p].
```

`LΩ_R` is p-completed derived de Rham over `Z_p`, without Hodge completion, and `Q_p(n)=Z_p(n)[1/p]`. A power of `p` depending only on `n` clears the denominators of `χ_n`; the associated cofibre is `(LΩ_R/Fil_H^n)[1/p]`. For p-torsion-free QRSP `R` and `n>0`, `χ_n` on `H⁰` is injective with image `A_crys(R/p)[1/p]^(φ=p^n)` in `LΩ_R[1/p]`. Its identification with the usual eigenspace inclusion is up to a nonzero scalar depending on `n`; Frobenius acts by `p^n`, and `χ_n` descends naturally through the characteristic-p de Rham–Witt comparison. No integral normalization or `n=0` eigenspace image assertion is imposed.

**Proof route.** On the QRSP basis take the [2n−1,2n] homotopy window of the rational trace square, identify graded TC by the trace comparison, and unfold; the uniform denominator then permits polynomial left Kan extension to the un-Hodge-completed de Rham target, with the finite Hodge quotient detecting the relative cofibre. The Frobenius-compatible characteristic-p lifting argument identifies the eigenspace map up to the multiplicative scalar `λ^n`, and the weight-one square at `O_C` forces `λ≠0`. (AMMN22 Construction 6.16 and Theorem 6.17, pp.44–45, Propositions 6.18 and 6.21, Lemma 6.19 and Corollary 6.20, pp.45–46, Corollary B.4; BS23-FCRYS Proposition 6.8, p.22.)

The API is `Prismatic.Beilinson.chi`, `chi_boundedDenominator`, `square`, `cofibre`, `chi_characteristicP_natural`, `chi_frobenius`, `chi_eigenspaceImage` and `chi_scalarNonzero`. The last two require `n>0`; all square and cofibre maps retain their functorial commutative diagrams. Expose the compatibility names `gradedBeilinsonSquare`, `gradedBeilinsonSquare_map`, `gradedBeilinsonSquare_zero`, `gradedBeilinsonSquare_cartesian`, `gradedBeilinsonSquare_integral` and `gradedBeilinsonSquare_local` for, respectively, the square, its naturality, weight-zero identity, cartesianness, bounded-denominator integral diagram and QRSP two-degree construction. These names alias the constructions above, with the same p-torsion-free quasisyntomic hypotheses and weight `n≥0`. The integral name asserts a commutative diagram whose rationalization is the square; it does not assert integral cartesianness.

*Needs:* Layers 2–3 (quasisyntomic descent, root covers, Nygaard filtration and §3.26), Layer 6 (§§6.8–6.9 syntomic fibres and Kan extension; characteristic-p crystalline and weight-one Kummer comparisons), AInfCohomology §AI.7.2 with the specific targets named above, DerivedModuleAlgebra §§1.1–1.9; DerivedDeRhamCohomology §§1.10–1.23, DerivedDeRhamCohomology Layer 2, DerivedDeRhamCohomology §§5.1–5.7, CrystallineCohomology §§CR.4.1–CR.4.8, CR.DDR.4.5, CR.DDR.5.18–CR.DDR.5.22, EnhancedDerivedSheaves E2 and EnhancedDerivedSheaves Layer E5:abstract.

**Checks.**

- At `n=0`, `Fil_H⁰LΩ=LΩ` and the Hodge quotient is zero; the two rational weight-zero syntomic terms are equivalent. No positive-weight eigenspace assertion is used here.
- For `R=O_C` and `n>0` the square gives `0→Q_p(n)→(B_crys⁺)^(φ=p^n)→B_dR⁺/Fil^n→0`, with the standard inclusion up to the nonzero scalar just specified.
- For `n=1` the Hodge quotient is `R[1/p]`, so the relative boundary lands in degree zero de Rham cohomology. A single suspension in place of `Σ²HC` would produce the wrong degree.
- A p-torsion-free smooth Z_p-algebra is covered by the square after p-completion. An arbitrary p-torsion ring is not substituted for `R` here: flatness of `R` over `Z_p` is what ensures that ordinary `R/p` has the required quasisyntomic cotangent amplitude in this construction.

**Gluing finite projective lattices.** Consume the finite-projective restriction of `AInf.linear_module_patching` from AInfCohomology §AI.2.1. Its compatibility alias `FilteredBundle.latticeGluing` is, for a commutative ring `R` and a nonzerodivisor `d`, the restriction equivalence

```text
Vect(R) ≃ Vect(R[1/d]) ×_(Vect(Rhat[1/d])) Vect(Rhat),
Rhat = lim_n R/d^n.
```

Morphisms are the supplied compatible pairs on the two charts. Prove that the alias respects tensor products and duals, and commutes with base change whenever the base-changed element remains a nonzerodivisor, using the induced map between completions. This is the affine gluing input for the completed lattices in §§7.0 and 7.17; it is distinct from faithfully flat descent. (Stacks, [Theorem 0BP2](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0BP2) and [Lemma 0BP6](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0BP6).)

**Checks.** For `R=Z_p` and `d=p` the completed chart is `R` itself and gluing returns the given lattice. For `d` a unit the completed chart is zero and restriction to `R[1/d]=R` is the identity. For `R=Z` and `d=p`, a rank-one transition `p^r` in `Q_p` glues the generic line and the completed lattice `p^rZ_p`; changing `r` changes the embedded lattice, although every resulting abstract Z-module is free of rank one. The nonzerodivisor hypothesis excludes `d=0` on a nonzero ring.

*Needs:* AInfCohomology §AI.2.1 (`AInf.linear_module_patching`); Mathlib `Module.Finite`, `Module.Projective`, `AdicCompletion`, `Localization.Away`; SchemeAndStackFoundations §1.1.

**Absolute curve and Robba comparison.** For `E=ℚ_p, F=C^♭` in Conventions, use AdicSpaces Layer 6's annulus coefficients `B^I` and `Y/φ^Z`, with `B` the functions on `Y`. Construct `X_FF=Proj(⊕_(m≥0)B^{φ=p^m})`, regular noetherian of dimension one, and the analytification equivalence between coherent sheaves on `X_FF` and φ-equivariant coherent sheaves on `Y`, restricting to vector bundles. Its completion at the untilt point ∞ is `B_dR⁺`. Restriction to a Frobenius-covering annulus window and gluing its translates gives the geometric extended-Robba/curve equivalence `FilteredBundle.robbaEquivalence` on AInfCohomology §AI.2.3's coefficients. Coherent sheaves correspond to finitely presented Robba modules with invertible Frobenius linearization; vector bundles correspond to finite projective ones. Retain torsion objects in the coherent comparison.

The geometric period norms are `|Σ[x_n]p^n|_ρ=sup |x_n|ρ^n`. Put `r=−log_pρ` and `κ=α/r` for `|ϖ|=p^(−α)`; germs as `ρ→0` correspond to `r→∞` and `κ→0` in the supplied annulus coordinates. Overlaps of windows give canonical independence. Identify the degree-m piece with `H⁰(Y/φ^Z,O(m))`, where `O(m)` descends the line with Frobenius `p^(−m)φ`; a section has coefficient eigenvalue `p^m`.

**Proof route.** The homogeneous localization charts of `Proj` construct the schematic curve. Compare their completed local rings with the analytic period completions. Annulus restriction and Frobenius gluing give the Robba comparison; positive twists give bundle GAGA, and the completed local-ring comparison includes torsion coherent sheaves. These constructions have no filtered-module or crystalline-representation prerequisite. (FF §6.5, Theorem 6.5.2, pp.204–206; Definition 1.8.1, p.37; Proposition 11.2.20, Corollary 11.2.22 and Remark 11.2.23, pp.327–328; Theorem 11.3.1, pp.330–332.)

The API is `FilteredBundle.schemeCurve`, `analyticEquivalence`, `robbaEquivalence` and `untiltCompletion`, with compatibility on morphisms, twists, duals and tensor products. These are the shared absolute carriers and equivalences for every filtered modification below.

*Needs:* `TauCetiRoadmap.AdicSpaces` Layers 0–6 (annuli and adic quotient); SchemeAndStackFoundations §0.3 (graded rings and Proj), §1.1 (vector-bundle descent); `TauCetiRoadmap.AlgebraicVectorBundles` Layers L0A–L0B; `TauCetiRoadmap.PadicPeriodRings` §§3 and 4.1; AInfCohomology §AI.2.3 (extended Robba coefficients).

**Checks.**

- Rank zero corresponds to the zero object; the unit Robba module descends to `O_(X_FF)`. The line with Frobenius multiplier `p⁻¹` descends to `O_(X_FF)(1)`, whose sections satisfy `φ(f)=pf`.
- Changing the annulus window gives the same Robba germ and descended object. A zero Frobenius matrix on a nonzero free module fails invertible linearization.
- At ∞, both completions are `B_dR⁺`. The nonzero skyscraper sheaf with completed stalk `B_dR⁺/t` remains in the coherent equivalence and lies outside its vector-bundle restriction; a comparison only of finite projectives would miss it.
- A nonzero positive-degree homogeneous section and any positive power of it define the same Proj chart and analytic nonvanishing locus.

**Filtered Frobenius bundle dictionary.** Use the absolute curve and comparisons above. Consume the classical Robba rings and slope/weak-admissibility theorems of FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §§R07.4.7–R07.4.9, Kisin's open-disc object of §R07.4.6, and AInfCohomology §AI.2.3's Fargues classification. Extend the supplied classical Robba object through its period embedding into the geometric coefficients, carrying the semilinear map and the untilt lattice. Require this extension to intertwine Frobenius and identify the two annulus conventions under `FilteredBundle.robbaEquivalence`. The filtered dictionary owns the modification, its completed-lattice identification and its prismatic comparison.

For a filtered φ-module `D` with finite exhaustive separated filtration, form the constant isocrystal bundle `E(D,φ_D)` and modify its lattice at ∞ to

```text
Λ_Fil = Fil⁰(D_K⊗_K B_dR) = Σ_i Fil^i D_K⊗_K t^(−i)B_dR⁺.
```

Beauville–Laszlo gluing defines a functorial tensor modification `E(D,φ_D,Fil)`, with degree `t_H(D)−t_N(D)`. Transport the book's untwisted filtration to `D⊗_(K₀,φ)K` by explicit Frobenius base change. The geometric bundle degree is the negative of the supplied classical Robba degree `t_N−t_H`; these signs are not identified.

Prove `FilteredBundle.prismaticIdentification`: the continuation of the F-crystal of §7.17 on `Y`, after geometric Robba extension, corresponds to this modification. On the complement of the Frobenius orbit of ∞ it is the constant isocrystal trivialization; at each orbit point the completed lattice is `Λ_Fil`, with transition `φ_D`. Consume weak admissibility `⇔` classical Robba slope zero from FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §R07.4.9 (transporting duals and arbitrary weights as in §7.17); the adapter yields `FilteredBundle.weaklyAdmissible_slopeZero` for the modified bundle. Consume AI.2.3's classification over the algebraically closed tilt to obtain `FilteredBundle.slopeZero_trivial`, hence a geometric φ-fixed basis. Neither conclusion invokes the crystalline-representation equivalence being proved later.

**Proof route.** On the shared absolute curve glue the specified completed lattice and compare it with §7.17 at the same orbit points; then apply the supplied slope-zero theorem and classification through the coefficient adapter. (FF Propositions 8.2.6–8.2.8 and Theorem 8.2.10, pp.236–238, Proposition 10.5.3, p.289, Proposition 10.5.6, p.290; BS23-FCRYS §6.4, pp.26–27.)

The API is `FilteredBundle.isocrystalBundle`, `modification`, `modification_completedLattice`, `degree`, `prismaticIdentification`, `FilteredBundle.weaklyAdmissible_slopeZero` and `FilteredBundle.slopeZero_trivial`, with compatibility on morphisms, duals and tensor products. `prismaticIdentification` requires the completed-lattice construction of `M(D)` below; the other dictionary statements have no prismatic essential-surjectivity prerequisite.

*Needs:* §7.0 (Absolute curve and Robba comparison); §7.0 (Gluing finite projective lattices); `TauCetiRoadmap.PadicPeriodRings` §§3 and 4.1; FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §§R07.3.1–R07.3.2, R07.4.6–R07.4.9; AInfCohomology §AI.2.3. The coefficient/lattice adapter is constructed here; slope zero and classification are the supplier inputs identified above.

**Checks.**

- Rank zero gives the zero bundle and the unique zero Frobenius module. `D=K₀` with `φ=1` and the filtration jumping at zero gives `O_(X_FF)`, whose Robba module has a fixed basis.
- In the book’s untwisted convention, `φ(e)=pe` and `Fil¹D_K=D_K`, `Fil²D_K=0` give `t_N=t_H=1` and `Λ_Fil=t^(−1)B_dR⁺e`. The unmodified line has degree −1; the modified line has degree zero. Using `t` instead of `t^(−1)` would give degree −2.
- A direct sum of rank-one objects with numerical degrees 1 and −1 has total degree zero and is not semistable. Equality of total Hodge and Newton degrees without the subobject inequalities does not imply triviality.
- The nonzero-slope line `O_(X_FF)(1)` is not covered by the triviality assertion. Algebraic closedness of `C^♭` is retained when applying classification.
- Changing the annulus window gives the same Robba germ and descended bundle; changing an integral Frobenius matrix to the zero matrix fails the invertible-semilinear hypothesis.
- The completion of the scheme at ∞ and the period completion on the analytic side are both `B_dR⁺`, and analytification preserves the specified lattice. Identifying Robba modules only with analytic bundles and applying an algebraic classification without GAGA would omit this comparison.

### 7.0b Almost sections of the Galois product

In the perfect-residue notation of §7.0a let `R=O_C⊗̂_{O_K}O_C` and `S=R_perfd`, taking `O_C` as base through the first factor. Put `A_inf=W(O_C^♭)` and `I=ker θ̃`. The comparisons below are functorial and Frobenius-compatible.

- `GaloisProduct.perfectoidization_sections`: The pairing `x⊗y↦(g↦xg(y))` induces an almost isomorphism `S→Cont(G_K,O_C)`, for the almost ideal `m_C⊂O_C`.
- `GaloisProduct.witt_sections`: It induces an almost isomorphism `Δ_{R,perf}→Cont(G_K,A_inf)`, for the idempotent ideal generated by [a], `a∈m_{C^♭}`. The function ring uses the (p,I)-adic topology on `A_inf`.
- `GaloisProduct.laurent_sections`: After derived I-inversion and p-completion the second comparison is an isomorphism. With the same order of operations, this holds on the iterated tensor-product Čech nerve, where `G_K` is replaced by `G_K^n`. Thus descent of a lattice after Laurent realization is continuous Galois descent.

**Proof route.** Integral perfectoidization (§2.31) makes `S` discrete perfectoid, since `R` is the p-completion of the filtered union of integral `O_C⊗_(O_K)O_L`, `L/K` finite inside K̄; §2.30 supplies its universal property and base independence. That property identifies generic diamonds, without asserting `R[1/p]=S[1/p]` as rings. Apply §6.0b’s product property and the pro-étale torsor `Spa(C)→Spa(K)` to identify the diamond with `G_K×Spa(C)` (FCRYS §6.4, pp.26–27).

**Proof route.** Removing pseudouniformizer torsion is almost, and root closedness makes `U_tf→U_tf^∘` almost; apply v-almost acyclicity to the generic perfectoid space to obtain the integral function comparison. Tilt and use the finite Witt-vector Verschiebung filtrations, followed by their inverse limit, to obtain the Witt comparison. If `J=([a]:a∈m_(C^♭))`, a generator `d` of `I` lies in J+(p), so the almost-zero cone modulo `p^n` is d^n-annihilated; I-inversion and then derived p-completion give the exact Laurent comparison, compatibly on the entire Čech nerve (FCRYS §6.4, pp.26–27; ECD Proposition 8.8, p.43).

(BS23-FCRYS, §6.4, pp.26–27, for the Galois product and its presentation; BS22, Corollary 8.12, pp.68–69, and Theorem 10.11, pp.82–83, for the local perfectoidization inputs; ECD, Proposition 8.8, p.43, for v-almost acyclicity. The generic-diamond product is constructed in §6.0b.)

*Needs:* PrismaticCohomology §2.31, PrismaticCohomology §2.30, §6.0b, PerfectoidSpaces P1.16–P1.18, P1.24–P1.25, PerfectoidSpaces P0, PerfectoidSpaces P1, PerfectoidSpaces P5, DiamondsAndVStacks D2/higher-v-acyclicity, DiamondsAndVStacks D6/spd-is-a-spatial-diamond, Mathlib `WittVector`.

**Checks.**

- `GaloisProduct.degree_zero`: On the identity Galois element the pairing is multiplication `x⊗y↦xy`; reversing the two projections changes the cocycle convention and must be tracked.
- `GaloisProduct.finite_quotient`: For a finite Galois quotient `H`, the generic function algebra is `∏_(h∈H)C` and its integral function ring is `∏_(h∈H)O_C`, with the permutation descent action.
- `GaloisProduct.almost_not_integral`: The map before I-inversion is asserted almost. An element killed by all [a] may be nonzero, so it cannot be discarded in an integral lattice argument.
- `GaloisProduct.inversion_order`: Exactness is obtained on the cone modulo `p^n` after I-inversion, and then by the derived limit. Omitting either inversion or completion does not follow from this proof.
- `GaloisProduct.nerve`: On three tensor factors the two descriptions of the coface pairing give `(g,h)↦xg(y)gh(z)`, so the comparison retains the descent cocycle.

### 7.1 Prismatic crystals in vector bundles and in perfect complexes

Let `X` be a p-adic formal scheme. A prismatic crystal in vector bundles on `X` is an object of `Vect(X_Δ, O_Δ) := lim_{(A,I) ∈ X_Δ} Vect(A)`: a rule `E` giving a finite projective A-module `E(A)` for every bounded prism `(A, I)` with a map `Spf(A/I) → X`, and for every map of prisms `f`: `(A, I) → (B, J)` over `X` an isomorphism of B-modules `c_f`: `E(A) ⊗_A B ≅ E(B)`, with `c_id = id` and `c_{g∘f} = c_g ∘ (c_f ⊗_B C)` for `(A,I) → (B,J) → (C,K)`. Equivalently the `c_f` are given by f-semilinear maps `E(A) → E(B)`, functorial in `f`, each exhibiting `E(B)` as the base change of `E(A)`. A morphism `E → E′` is a family of A-linear maps `E(A) →` E′(A) commuting with the transition maps. In the same way `D_perf(X_Δ, O_Δ) := lim D_perf(A)` (a limit of ∞-categories, transition functors `B ⊗^L_A −`), and for the two coefficient systems `O′ = O_Δ[1/p]^∧_{I_Δ}` (value `A[1/p]^∧_I`, the I-adic completion of `A[1/p]`) and `O′ = O_Δ[1/I_Δ]^∧_p` (value `A[1/I]^∧_p`, the derived p-completion of the ring of functions on `Spec A ∖ V(I)`) one puts `Vect(X_Δ, O′) := lim Vect(O′(A))` and `D_perf(X_Δ, O′) := lim D_perf(O′(A))`. Structure: the unit `O_Δ (A ↦` A); tensor product (`E ⊗` E′)(A) `= E(A) ⊗_A` E′(A) and duals `E^∨(A) = Hom_A(E(A)`, A), making `Vect(X_Δ, O_Δ)` rigid symmetric monoidal; the evaluation functors `ev_{(A,I)}`: `Vect(X_Δ, O_Δ) → Vect(A)`; the pullback `g^*`: `Vect(X_Δ, O_Δ) → Vect(Y_Δ, O_Δ)` along `g`: `Y → X`, `(g^*E)(A, I, Spf(A/I) → Y) = E(A, I, Spf(A/I) → Y → X)`; and base change `Vect(X_Δ, O_Δ) → Vect(X_Δ, O′)`, `E ↦ (A ↦ E(A) ⊗_A` O′(A)).

`X` is an arbitrary p-adic formal scheme; no boundedness or quasi-syntomicity is needed for the definition. In BS23-FCRYS v2 the definition is the displayed formula opening §4, the sheaf-theoretic notions are Notation 2.1, and their agreement is Proposition 2.7 (§7.2). Completions: `A[1/p]^∧_I` is the classical I-adic completion; it agrees with the derived one because `I` is locally generated by a nonzerodivisor of `A[1/p]`. Put `B=A[1/I]`, `C_cl=lim_(n≥1) B/p^n` and `C_der=Rlim_(n≥1)(B⊗ᴸ_Z Z/p^n)`. The notation `A[1/I]^∧_p` means `C_der` for both Vect and `D_perf` throughout Layer 7. For a connective derived coefficient algebra, Vect is the full ∞-category of finite locally free modules, locally retracts of finite sums of the coefficient algebra. Its objects need not be discrete modules. Scalar extension and limits retain their homotopies; an ordinary category is obtained only after taking the homotopy category of the coherent limit. In particular, the finite-level description is `Vect(C_der)≃lim_n Vect(B⊗ᴸ_Z Z/p^n)`, with derived quotients and coherent morphisms.

If `B` has bounded p-power torsion, the canonical map `C_der→C_cl` is an equivalence of coefficient algebras, and hence identifies their Vect and perfect-complex categories (BMS2 Lemma 4.6; DerivedModuleAlgebra §§1.4–1.8). This applies when A is p-torsion-free, including transversal, perfect and Breuil–Kisin prisms. For arbitrary bounded prisms the two coefficient systems remain distinct: `Vect(C_cl)≃lim_n Vect(B/p^n)` is the classical effectivity statement (Stacks [0D4B](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0D4B)), without an identification with `Vect(C_der)`.

(BS23-FCRYS, Section 4, opening display; p.12; BS23-FCRYS, Notation 2.1; p. 5; BS23-FCRYS, Definition 2.3; p. 5.)

*Needs:* PrismaticCohomologyPartII §4.1, PerfectoidSpaces §P4.15.2, PrismaticCohomology §0.73, PrismaticCohomology §0.79, PrismaticCohomology §0.81, Mathlib `Module.Finite`, Mathlib `Module.Projective`, Mathlib `TensorProduct`, Mathlib `AdicCompletion`; DerivedModuleAlgebra §§1.1–1.9, EnhancedDerivedSheaves §§E1.1–E1.4, E3 and E5:animation.4 (modules over derived coefficient algebras). The completion boundary example uses PrismaticCohomology §0.89.

- `Prismatic.FCrystal.Crystal`: A crystal in vector bundles over a diagram of ordinary rings `O`: `C →` CommRing: finite projective O(c)-modules `M(c)` with functorial semilinear transition maps that are base changes. For a derived coefficient diagram use the coherent limit of finite locally free module ∞-categories.
- `Prismatic.FCrystal.Crystal.Hom`: For ordinary coefficients, O(c)-linear maps `M(c)→M′(c)` commuting with transitions; for derived coefficients, the mapping space in the coherent limit.
- `Prismatic.FCrystal.Crystal.unit`: The unit crystal `c ↦ O(c)`, with transition maps the ring maps.
- `Prismatic.FCrystal.Crystal.tensor`: The tensor product of two crystals, with (`E ⊗` E′)(c) `≅ E(c) ⊗_{O(c)}` E′(c).
- `Prismatic.FCrystal.Crystal.pullback`: Restriction of a crystal along a functor `D → C`; for `D = Y_Δ → C = X_Δ` this is `g^*`.
- `Prismatic.FCrystal.Crystal.baseChange`: Base change along a map of coefficient diagrams `O→O′`, with values `O′(c)⊗ᴸ_(O(c))E(c)`; for ordinary coefficients and finite projective modules this is ordinary scalar extension.
- `Prismatic.FCrystal.Crystal.Iso`: An isomorphism of ordinary crystals, or an equivalence in the coherent crystal category for derived coefficients.
- `Prismatic.FCrystal.Crystal.dual`: The dual crystal has `E^∨(A)=Hom_A(E(A),A)` for ordinary coefficients and the derived module dual for derived coefficients; base-change compatibility follows from finite local freeness. Evaluation and coevaluation satisfy the triangle identities, making crystals rigid symmetric monoidal.

**Checks.**

- For a ring map `g:R→S`, scalar extension on endomorphisms of the free rank-one module is `g` under evaluation at 1. It is injective exactly when `g` is injective.
- For every prime, including 2, use the finite-chain modules of Part I §0.89 over `T=Z_p[[d]]`: generators `e_(i,0),…,e_(i,i)`, relations `p e_(i,j)=d e_(i,j+1)` and `p e_(i,i)=0`. Set `M=⊕_i M_i`, `N=lim_r M/d^rM` and `A=T⊕N` with `N²=0`. Give T Frobenius `φ(d)=d^p+p`, `φ|Z_p=id`, and put `δ(a,m)=(δ_T(a),−a^(p−1)m)`, so `φ(a,m)=(φ_T(a),0)`. The δ identities hold directly, including with p-torsion. The module embeddings and filtration identities of §0.89 give d-regularity, derived `(p,d)`-completeness and `A/d=Z_p⊕M/dM` with torsion killed by `p`. Since `δ(d)=1` and `p=φ(d)−d^p`, this is a bounded prism.
  For `B=A[1/d]` the Milnor sequence is `0→lim¹_n(B[p^n],p)→H⁰(C_der)→C_cl→0` (Stacks [07KX](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/07KX)). The class represented by `a_n=d^(−2n)e_(n−1,0)` is nonzero: a solution `a_n=b_n−p b_(n+1)` would force the i-th coordinate of `b_1` to be `d^(−i−2)e_(i,i)`. These coordinates have no uniform bound on negative d-denominators, whereas every element of `N[1/d]` has such a bound. Also `lim_n(B[p^n],p)=0`, because its torsion embeds in the product of the localized `M_i`, each of bounded p-exponent. Thus `C_der` is discrete in this example, yet `H⁰(C_der)→C_cl` has a nonzero kernel. Scalar extension on the unit’s endomorphisms is not faithful. Discreteness alone does not justify the classical quotient tower.
- If `B` is p-torsion-free, every derived quotient `B⊗ᴸ_Z Z/p^n` is discrete, so the comparison with `C_cl` identifies both categories and all morphisms. Bounded p-power torsion also suffices for the comparison of the limits, even when individual derived quotients have degree `−1` terms.
- Along the diagonal `R→R×R`, the unit transition is `x↦(x,x)`, and its scalar extension is the rank-one `(R×R)`-module `R×R`. For `R=Z_3`, using a single factor would discard one component.
- The identity of a module exhibits it as its own base change along the identity ring map.
- On the one-object diagram with coefficient ring `Z_3`, the zero endomorphism of the unit module fails `IsBaseChange Z_3`; linearity alone does not give a crystal transition.
- Over `(Z_p,(p))`, the compatible quotient module `F_p` is not projective over `Z_p`: a splitting would embed its nonzero p-torsion into the p-torsion-free ring `Z_p`. Thus the Hodge–Tate quotient is not a crystal in vector bundles.

**Proof route.** For diagrams of ordinary rings, define compatible families with transition data tested by Mathlib `IsBaseChange` after restricting scalars; functoriality is the cocycle condition (BS23-FCRYS §4, first display). For derived coefficients use the enhanced module categories, derived scalar extension and their coherent limit; finite local freeness is imposed in that limit.

### 7.2 Descent for prismatic crystals

Let `X` be a p-adic formal scheme. (1) The assignment `(A, I) ↦ Vect(A)` on `X_Δ` is a sheaf of categories for the flat topology, and the natural functor `lim_{(A,I) ∈ X_Δ} Vect(A) → Vect(X_Δ, O_Δ)` to vector bundles on the ringed topos `(Shv(X_Δ), O_Δ)` (locally of the form `P ⊗ O` for `P` finite projective) is an equivalence; the same holds with `D_perf` in place of Vect. (2) The same two statements hold for `O_Δ[1/p]^∧_{I_Δ}` and for the sheaf of connective derived coefficient algebras `O_Δ[1/I_Δ]^∧_p` of §7.1. In the latter case perfect modules and finite locally free modules are defined over that derived sheaf, with derived scalar extension. (3) Čech form: if `(B, J) ∈ X_Δ` covers the final object of `Shv(X_Δ)` and `(B^•, J^•)` is its Čech nerve (the cosimplicial prism of iterated coproducts in the category of prisms over `X`), then `Vect(X_Δ, O_Δ) ≃ lim(Vect(B^0) ⇉ Vect(B^1)` ⇶ `Vect(B^2)`): a crystal is a finite projective B-module `M` with an isomorphism `p_1^*M ≅ p_2^*M` over `B^1` satisfying the cocycle condition over `B^2`. The same description applies to the discrete I-completed coefficients. For the derived Laurent coefficients and for `D_perf`, use the full cosimplicial limit, retaining every coherence also for Vect; a module, an isomorphism and an ordinary cocycle alone do not specify that limit.

In (3) the Čech nerve exists when the iterated bounded-prism coproducts over `X` exist; §7.12 checks the two covers of `Spf(O_K)`. The punctured descent input is owned here, rather than assumed from ordinary SchemeAndStackFoundations §1.1 descent.

- `Prismatic.Crystal.puncturedCompleteDescent`: for connective `E∞ R` and finitely generated `J⊂π₀R`, `S↦APerf(Spec(Ŝ_J)∖V(J))` is a hypercomplete sheaf on connective R-algebras for J-completely faithfully flat covers, as are its connective, perfect and fixed Tor-amplitude `[a,b]` subcategories; `Perf^[0,0]` gives Vect. Completion and Čech tensors are derived. Complete faithful flatness means t-exact and conservative extension on J-torsion modules. This is Mathew Theorem 7.8, p.28, with Definition 7.1, p.27; Theorem 5.8, p.18, instead concerns universal descent and is not the locator for complete flatness.

**Checks.**

- `Prismatic.Crystal.puncturedCompleteDescent_extremeIdeals`: `J=0` gives an empty complement; `J=R` has zero completed ring and again the zero category.
- `Prismatic.Crystal.puncturedCompleteDescent_principal`: `R=S=Z_p`, `J=(p)` gives `Perf(Q_p)`, with Vect its finite-dimensional vector spaces and identity descent.
- `Prismatic.Crystal.puncturedCompleteDescent_needsFlatness`: `Z_p→F_p` is not p-completely flat: `F_p⊗ᴸ_(Z_p)F_p` has nonzero `Tor₁`. Its punctured completed target is empty, so it cannot descend the nonzero unit on `Spec(Q_p)`.

(BS23-FCRYS, Proposition 2.7 and its proof; p. 6; BS23-FCRYS, Theorem 2.2; p. 5; BS23-FCRYS, Example 2.8; p. 7.)

*Needs:* §7.1, PrismaticCohomology §2.29, PrismaticCohomologyPartII §4.1, PrismaticCohomology §1.1, PrismaticCohomology §0.81, SchemeAndStackFoundations §§1.1, 1.18–1.22, DerivedModuleAlgebra §§1.1–1.9; DerivedDeRhamCohomology §§1.10–1.23, EnhancedDerivedSheaves E3.

**Proof route.** For the punctured interface, descend in the torsion-complete ambient category and use locality of weak almost perfection, then dualizability and fixed Tor amplitude (Mathew Propositions 7.3 and 7.7, Theorem 7.8, pp.27–28). Apply it to the two punctured completed coefficient systems, retaining the derived quotient towers in the Laurent case. Finite local freeness is detected by Tor-amplitude `[0,0]` after reduction and then descended in the enhanced category. For integral coefficients use finite-level flat descent and complete-projective effectivity (FCRYS Proposition 2.7, p.6). Ordinary quotient effectivity is used for Laurent coefficients only under the bounded-torsion comparison of §7.1.

### 7.3 Crystals on the prismatic site and on the quasi-syntomic site

Let `X` be a quasi-syntomic p-adic formal scheme, `X_qsyn` its quasi-syntomic site and `X_qrsp ⊂ X_qsyn` the full subcategory of `Spf(R) → X` with `R` quasiregular semiperfectoid; `Δ_•` is the sheaf of rings `R ↦ Δ_R` on `X_qsyn` (defined on `X_qrsp`) and `I ⊂ Δ_•` its ideal sheaf. (1) `lim_{R ∈ X_qrsp} Vect(Δ_R) ≃ Vect(X_qsyn, Δ_•)`. (2) There is a natural equivalence `Vect(X_Δ, O_Δ) ≃ Vect(X_qsyn, Δ_•)`, induced by pullback along the map of ringed topoi `ν`: `(Shv(X_Δ), O_Δ) → (Shv(X_qsyn), Δ_•)` determined by `R ↦ Δ_R`. (3) Both statements hold with `D_perf` in place of Vect, and with `(O_Δ[1/p]^∧_{I_Δ}, Δ_•[1/p]^∧_I)` or `(O_Δ[1/I_Δ]^∧_p, Δ_•[1/I]^∧_p)` in place of `(O_Δ, Δ_•)`. (4) Concretely, for `X = Spf(R)` affine and `R → S` a quasi-syntomic cover with `S` quasiregular semiperfectoid, with p-completed Čech nerve `S^•`, both sides are `lim Vect(Δ_{S^•})`.

`X` is quasi-syntomic (DerivedDeRhamCohomology Layer 5). For `R` in `X_qrsp` the prism `Δ_R` is bounded. The topos `Shv(X_qsyn)` equals `Shv(X_qrsp)` (BMS2 Proposition 4.31, Variant 4.35; DerivedDeRhamCohomology Layer 5).
