**Proof route.** Faithfulness: `(𝔖, (E))` covers the final object of `Shv(X_Δ)` (§7.12). The functor `𝒟`: `Vect^φ(𝔖⟨I/p⟩[1/p]) → MF^φ(K)`.

### 7.25 Comparison with Kisin's modules of finite E-height

Notation as in §7.24; Kisin's objects are those of the construction of FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory (Layer R07.4), with Hodge–Tate weights normalised by `HT(χ_cyc) = +1`. (1) Rings: `𝔖`, `φ`, `E(u)` are those of §R07.4.1 (`Sfrak`, `OE`, `toWittR`), and the ring `𝒪_ℰ` there is `𝔖[1/E(u)]^∧_p`. Embeddings: R07.4 uses `ι_K`: `𝔖 → A_inf`, `u ↦ [π̃]`, compatible with `θ`; the map of prisms `(𝔖, (E)) → (A_inf, ker θ̃)` of §7.12 is `ι = φ ∘ ι_K = ι_K ∘ φ_𝔖 (u ↦ [π̃]^p`, Frobenius on `W(k)`). For `M ∈ Vect^φ(𝔖)`, `M ⊗_{𝔖,ι} W(C^♭) = φ^*(M ⊗_{𝔖,ι_K} W(C^♭))`, and `φ_M` is a φ- and `G_{K_∞}`-equivariant isomorphism from it to `M ⊗_{𝔖,ι_K} W(C^♭)`; the étale realisation of Layer 7 is identified with Fontaine's functor through this isomorphism. (2) Modules: the effective objects of `Vect^φ(𝔖)` are the objects of `Mod^φ_{/𝔖}` (finite free, finite E-height) of §R07.4.2 (`KisinModule`); every object of `Vect^φ(𝔖)` becomes effective after replacing `φ_M` by `E(u)^h φ_M`, which changes no Hom group. `Vect^φ(𝔖[1/E]^∧_p)` is the category of finite free étale φ-modules over `𝒪_ℰ`, and the equivalence with `Rep_{Z_p}(G_{K_∞})` of §7.23 (3) is Fontaine's covariant functor of §R07.4.5 (`kummerEtalePhiModules`); Kisin's contravariant `V_𝔖(𝔐) = Hom_{𝔖,φ}(𝔐, 𝔖^ur)` is the dual of the étale realisation of `𝔐`. Under this dictionary statement (2) of §7.23, on effective objects, is §R07.4.11 (`kisinEtaleFullFaithfulness`). (3) Lattices: for `L ∈ Rep^crys_{Z_p}(G_K)`, `D_𝔖(L)` is effective exactly when the Hodge–Tate weights of `L[1/p]` are `≤ 0`; in that case `D_𝔖(L)` is the 𝔖-lattice of finite E-height in the étale φ-module of `L|_{G_{K_∞}}`, unique by §R07.4.12 (`finiteHeightLattices`) (1), so `D_𝔖(L)` is Kisin's module `𝔐` with `V_𝔖(𝔐) ≅ L^∨|_{G_{K_∞}}` (§R07.4.13 (`semistableFiniteHeight`) `(b)` for the lattice `L^∨`, of weights `≥ 0`). For general `L` twist by `O_Δ{n}`. (4) Consequently §7.24 (1) and (2) are integral forms, for all Hodge–Tate weights, of §R07.4.10 (`kisinCrystallineEmbedding`) restricted to `N = 0` and of §R07.4.14 (`crystallineRestrictionFullFaithfulness`).

The comparison rests on the following targets of R07.4, with the following mathematical content: the Kummer-tower field of norms and Fontaine's étale φ-module equivalence for `G_{K_∞}` (§R07.4.5 (`kummerEtalePhiModules`)), modules of finite E-height (§R07.4.2 (`KisinModule`), §R07.4.6 (`phiNNablaModules`)), the slope-zero characterisation of weak admissibility (§R07.4.9 (`weaklyAdmissibleSlopeZero`)), and the lattice functor with full faithfulness of restriction to `G_{K_∞}` (§R07.4.12 (`finiteHeightLattices`), §R07.4.13 (`semistableFiniteHeight`), §R07.4.14 (`crystallineRestrictionFullFaithfulness`)). The arbitrary-weight rational restriction theorem is imported exactly as stated; its integral lattice consequence and the comparison of period realizations are proved below. The source's Remark 7.11 deduces the comparison from "Corollary 7.10"; what the argument uses is the full faithfulness of `Vect^φ(𝔖) → Rep_{Z_p}(G_{K_∞})`, i.e. Theorem 7.2. Use R07.4 as the lower-layer source of these coefficient objects.

(BS23-FCRYS, Remark 7.11; p. 33; BS23-FCRYS, Introduction, Section 1.2; pp.3–4.)

*Needs:* §7.24, §7.23, §7.14, §7.4, FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory R07.4, FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §R07.4.1 (`Sfrak`, `OE`, `toWittR`), FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §R07.4.2 (`KisinModule`), FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §R07.4.5 (`kummerEtalePhiModules`), FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §R07.4.11 (`kisinEtaleFullFaithfulness`), FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §R07.4.12 (`finiteHeightLattices`), FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §R07.4.13 (`semistableFiniteHeight`), FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §R07.4.10 (`kisinCrystallineEmbedding`), FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §R07.4.14 (`crystallineRestrictionFullFaithfulness`).

**Proof route.** Use `E(u)≡u^e` mod `p` to identify completed localizations, and field-of-norms descent plus Artin–Schreier theory to identify the integral étale realization over `𝒪̂_(ℰ^ur)` and `W(C^♭)`. The resulting map is an isomorphism because its finite-free modules agree modulo `p` and hence by complete Nakayama, with the prescribed G_(K∞)-action. This is the coefficient-extension compatibility used in (1), not an identification of the two coefficient rings (FCRYS Remark 7.11, p.33; FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §R07.4.5 (`kummerEtalePhiModules`)).

**Proof route.** Effective objects agree by definition, while for arbitrary finite-free objects a common power of `E(u)` makes both Frobenius matrices and their inverses integral. Transport full faithfulness after this common height adjustment and undo it. The argument concerns the localized Frobenius category `Vect^φ(𝔖)`, and does not assert every object has nonnegative E-height (FCRYS Theorem 7.2, pp.28–29; FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §R07.4.11 (`kisinEtaleFullFaithfulness`)).

**Proof route.** In the effective range, the Hodge–Tate calculation and the supplier’s finite-height lattice uniqueness identify the prismatic lattice with the contravariant Kisin lattice of `L^∨`; transport arbitrary weights by a common Tate twist and its inverse, using effective full faithfulness for independence. For (4), rational restriction full faithfulness extends `f` uniquely, and the extension preserves the original lattices because its underlying linear map is `f⊗Q_p`. The fully faithful étale-module realization identifies the two Breuil–Kisin functors (FCRYS Remark 7.11, p.33; FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §R07.4.12 (`finiteHeightLattices`) and §R07.4.14 (`crystallineRestrictionFullFaithfulness`)).

**Checks.**

- `KisinDictionary.integral_fixed_points`: The unit module has Frobenius-fixed points `Z_p` over both `𝒪̂_(ℰ^ur)` (the p-completed maximal unramified coefficient ring of FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §§R07.4.1, R07.4.5) and `W(C^♭)`. Their fraction fields have fixed points `Q_p`, so they cannot replace the integral coefficient rings in the lattice realization.
- `KisinDictionary.duality`: An effective module realizing `L` covariantly realizes `L^∨` under Kisin's Hom convention. In particular `𝔖{−1}` corresponds covariantly to `Z_p(−1)`, not `Z_p(1)`.
- `KisinDictionary.twist_independence`: Identifying `L(−n)` and then undoing the twist agrees with using `L(−n−1)`; the comparison takes place after a common effective twist.
- `KisinDictionary.negative_height`: `𝔖{1}` has Frobenius with an E-pole and lies in `Vect^φ(𝔖)`, while its inverse is effective; an all-weight dictionary confined to effective Kisin modules fails this example.
- `KisinDictionary.lattice_fullness`: Multiplication by `p^{-1}` on `Q_p` is G_K-equivariant but does not define a map `Z_p→Z_p`. Integral full faithfulness compares maps already preserving the specified lattices.

### 7.26 Failure of full faithfulness with mod p coefficients

Let `K = Q_p` and `X = Spf(Z_p)`. The invertible F-crystal `E = O_Δ{p−1}/p ∈ Vect^φ(X_Δ, O_Δ/p)` has étale realisation `T(E) =` Z/p(p−1) `≅ Z/p`, the trivial local system, but `E` is not isomorphic to the unit object `O_Δ/p`; in fact `E ≅ I_Δ^{-1}/p` as O_Δ/p-modules, and `I_Δ/p` is a non-trivial invertible O_Δ/p-module. Hence the étale realisation `Vect^φ(X_Δ, O_Δ/p) → Vect(X_Δ, O_Δ[1/I_Δ]/p)^{φ=1}` is not fully faithful, and the analogue of §7.22 with mod `p` coefficients is false. More generally the layer makes no claim that finite locally free prismatic F-crystals with torsion or derived coefficients classify torsion or derived Galois representations.

`X = Spf(Z_p)`; any prime `p`. The source attributes the failure to the fact that sections of vector bundles on `Spec(A_inf/p)` change when the closed point is removed, unlike on `Spec(A_inf)` (Kedlaya), which is what full faithfulness in Fargues' theorem uses. The source refers the Sen-operator computation to Bhatt–Lurie §3.5; use the Sen description of §§4.13–4.14.

(BS23-FCRYS, Remark 5.8; p. 18; BS23-FCRYS, Introduction, footnote to Theorem 1.2; p. 2.)

*Needs:* §7.5, §7.9, §7.22, PrismaticCohomologyPartII §3.9, PrismaticCohomologyPartII §4.13, PrismaticCohomologyPartII §4.14, PrismaticCohomologyPartII §4.10.

**Proof route.** `T` is symmetric monoidal and `T(O_Δ{1}) = Z_p(1)` (§7.9), so `T(E) =` Z/p(p−1), which is trivial because `χ_cyc^{p−1} ≡ 1` modulo `p`. `O_Δ{p}/p ≅ φ^*O_Δ{1}/p` because the Frobenius pullback of an invertible module over an F_p-algebra is its p-th tensor power, and `φ^*O_Δ{1} ≅ I_Δ^{-1} ⊗ O_Δ{1}` (§3.9); hence `E = O_Δ{p}/p ⊗ O_Δ{−1}/p ≅ I_Δ^{-1}/p`.

### Examples

For `K=Q_p`, the unit prismatic F-crystal realizes the trivial `Z_p` representation. The twist `O_Δ{1}` realizes `Z_p(1)` with `HT=+1`; the effective Kisin module is obtained after twisting into weights `≤0`. On the Breuil–Kisin prism the map `u↦[π^♭]^p` uses `θ̃`, whereas `u↦[π^♭]` uses `θ`. The two coefficient extensions are identified by `φ_M` before comparing their fixed points. Modulo `p`, `O_Δ{p−1}/p` realizes the trivial character but is not the unit crystal, including `p=2`. Hence the integral full-faithfulness theorem cannot be transferred to mod-p crystals.

**§7.2.** For `X = Spf(O_K)` and the Breuil–Kisin prism: a crystal is a finite free 𝔖-module with a descent isomorphism over `𝔖^(1)` and the cocycle condition over `𝔖^(2)`. For a faithfully flat map of bounded prisms `A → B` the sequence `Vect(A) → Vect(B) ⇉ Vect(B ⊗̂_A B)` is an equaliser of categories. The statement for `O_Δ[1/I_Δ]^∧_p` does not follow from flat descent of modules alone: `A[1/I]^∧_p → B[1/I]^∧_p` is not flat in general, and Drinfeld–Mathew is used.

**§7.3.** For `X = Spf(O_K)`: `Vect(X_Δ, O_Δ) ≃ lim(Vect(A_inf) ⇉ Vect(Δ_{O_C ⊗̂ O_C})` ⇶ `Vect(Δ_{O_C ⊗̂ O_C ⊗̂ O_C})`), completed tensor products over `O_K`. For `X = Spf(R)` with `R` quasiregular semiperfectoid both sides are `Vect(Δ_R)`.

**§7.5.** The unit `(O_Δ, φ)` is effective; `O_Δ{−1}` is effective and `O_Δ{1}` is not. On a quasi-syntomic F_p-scheme the notion is that of an F-crystal on the crystalline site (§7.10). For `X = Spf(R)`, `R` quasiregular semiperfectoid, `Vect^φ(X_Δ, O_Δ) = Vect^φ(Δ_R)`.

**§7.6.** For `X = Spf(O_C)`, with `C` complete and algebraically closed of characteristic 0, the category is `Vect(W(C^♭))^{φ=1}`, equivalent to `Vect(Z_p)` by taking φ-fixed points. For `X` of characteristic `p` the category is zero. A crystal over `O_Δ[1/I_Δ]^∧_p[1/p]` with `φ^*E ≅ E` is in general not a Laurent F-crystal up to isogeny: over `O_C` these are all F-isocrystals over `C^♭`, of which only those of slope 0 arise (the source's Remark 3.9).

**§7.7.** `S = F_p`: the right side is perfect F_p-complexes equipped with an automorphism, matching bounded lisse complexes on `Spec(F_p)` and their continuous Frobenius action. The automorphism data cannot be omitted. `S = k` a separably closed field: every `(M, α)` is trivial, `M ≅ M^{φ=1} ⊗_{F_p} k` (Lang). `S = F_p[x]`: on the rank-two free module take `Φ(e₁) = e₁` and `Φ(e₂) = e₂ + x e₁`. Its linearisation is invertible. A fixed basis requires a solution of `a^p − a = −x`, giving a nontrivial Artin–Schreier cover. The multiplier `1 + x` would not define an invertible rank-one Frobenius. The statement fails for finitely generated modules that are not projective if the isomorphism `α` is replaced by an arbitrary map.

**§7.8.** `X = Spf(O_C)`, with `C` complete and algebraically closed of characteristic 0: the category of Laurent F-crystals is `Vect(Z_p)` (taking φ-fixed points of modules over `W(C^♭)`). `X` of characteristic `p`: both sides are zero. `X =` the p-completion of `P^1` over `Z_p`: de Jong's Q_p-local system with monodromy `SL_2(Q_p)` is not in `Loc_{Z_p}(X_η) ⊗ Q_p`. Analogue on the special fibre (the source's Remark 3.11): `D_perf(X_Δ, O_Δ)^{φ=1} ≃ D^b_lisse(X_{p=0}, Z_p)`.

**§7.9.** `T(O_Δ)` is the constant local system `Z_p`; `T(O_Δ{1}) = Z_p(1)`. For `X` of characteristic `p`, `T = 0`; in particular `T` is not faithful there. Over `Spf(O_C)`, `T` is faithful but not full: `Hom(O_Δ{1}, O_Δ) = (μA_inf)^{φ=1} = 0`, while the realisations `Z_p(1)` and `Z_p` are isomorphic Z_p-modules (there is no Galois action over `O_C`). In the other direction the inclusion `A_inf ⊂ μ^{-1}A_inf = A_inf{1}` is a morphism whose realisation is an isomorphism although it is not one. `T` commutes with proper smooth pushforward of the structure sheaf (the source's Example 4.9), through the Artin–Schreier sequence on `X_{η,proét}`; this is the étale comparison §6.4 with coefficients and is described as a consistency check, a comparison with coefficients.

**§7.10.** `D_crys(O_Δ)` is the unit F-crystal; `D_crys` preserves ranks and tensor products. For `X = Spf(O_K)`: `D_crys(O_Δ{n})` is `W(k)` with Frobenius `p^{-n}φ`. On `Spec(F_p)`: prismatic F-crystals are finite free Z_p-modules `M` with an automorphism of `M[1/p]`.

**§7.11.** `R = O_C`: `A_inf → W(C^♭)` is injective. `R = O_C ⊗̂_{O_K} O_C` and its triple analogue: `T` is faithful on every term of the Čech nerve of `Spf(O_C) → Spf(O_K)`. `R = O_C/p`: `Δ_R = A_crys`, `I = (p)`, and the étale realisation is zero, so faithfulness fails without p-torsion-freeness.

**§7.12.** `K = Q_p`, `π = p`: `𝔖 = Z_p[[u]]`, `E = u − p`; `𝔖^{(1)} = Z_p[[u`, v]]{(u `−` v)/(u `− p)}^∧`. For `K = Q_p(p^{1/p})` and `π = p^{1/p}`, `E = u^p − p`. Under the fixed shifted embedding `u↦[π^♭]^p`, `E` maps to a unit multiple of `[p]_q`, generating `ker θ̃`. Since `φ(u−p)=E(u)`, the same embedding carries u−p to a unit multiple of `φ⁻¹([p]_q)=[p]_{q^{1/p}}`, generating `ker θ` (BS23-FCRYS Example 7.8, p.31). Thus `θ̃(u−p)=π−p≠0`: u−p does not generate the source prism ideal in this ramified example. The Breuil–Kisin prism is not perfect; the A_inf-prism is its image under a map to a perfect prism, not its perfection (the perfection is `A_inf` of the completion of `K(π^{1/p^∞})`).

**§7.13.** `M = A_inf` with `φ`: `(T, F) = (Z_p, B_dR^+)`. `M = A_inf{1} ≅ μ^{-1}A_inf`: `T = Z_p(1)` and `F = ξ̃^{-1}·(T ⊗ B_dR^+)`, a lattice different from `T ⊗ B_dR^+`; so `A_inf` and `A_inf{1}` are not isomorphic although their realisations are. The equivalence is not asserted for finitely presented torsion Breuil–Kisin–Fargues modules.

**§7.14.** `E = O_Δ`: `T = Z_p`, `D_crys = K_0` with `φ`. `E = O_Δ{1}`: `T = Z_p(1)`, `E(W(k)) = W(k)` with `p^{-1}φ`; this fixes the normalisation of `D_crys` relative to PadicPeriodRings §4.2. A ramified character of finite order is de Rham but not crystalline, so by (1) its lattice is not the realisation of a prismatic F-crystal on `Spf(O_K)`.

**§7.15.** `Hom(O_Δ, O_Δ{n}) = 0` for `n ≠ 0`, matching `Hom_{G_K}(Z_p, Z_p(n)) = 0`. `End(O_Δ) = Z_p`. The statement fails with O_Δ/p-coefficients (§7.26).

**§7.18.** `R = O_C`: both sides are one-dimensional over `Q_p`, equal to `Q_p(n)` as G_K-modules. `R = O_C`: the sequence `0 → Q_p(n) → (B_crys^+)^{φ=p^n} → B_dR^+/Fil^n → 0` is the plus form of the fundamental exact sequence (TauCetiRoadmap.PadicPeriodRings §4.3 states the `φ = 1` form), which checks the suspension dictionary. For `R = O_C` and `n ≥ 1` the map `c` is not surjective: its cokernel is `B_dR^+/Fil^n`.

**§7.19.** `M = A_inf`: the descent datum is multiplication by a φ-fixed unit of `Δ_R⟨I/p⟩[1/p]`, which lies in `Δ_R[1/p]`. `M = A_inf{1}`: in the bases `(q_i − 1)^{-1}` the datum of the crystal `O_Δ{1}` is a unit `c` of `Δ_R` with `φ(c) = c·[p]_{q_2}/[p]_{q_1}`. Without a Frobenius structure the extension fails: a general element of `Δ_R⟨I/p⟩[1/p]` is not in `Δ_R[1/p]`.

**§7.20.** `D = K_0` with `φ` and trivial filtration: `M′ = A_inf`. `D` of dimension one with `φ_D = p^{-1}φ` and filtration jumping at −1 (weakly admissible): `M′ = A_inf{1}`. `D` of dimension one with `φ_D = p^{-1}φ` and filtration jumping at 0 (not weakly admissible): the bundle on `X_FF` has degree +1 under the convention deg `E = t_H − t_N (t_H=0` and `t_N=−1`), so it is not trivial, and `M(D)(Y)` does not extend over `A_inf`.

**§7.21.** `𝔐(Z_p) = O_Δ` and `𝔐(Z_p(n)) = O_Δ{n}`. rk `𝔐(L) = rk_{Z_p} L`, and `𝔐(L ⊗ L′) ≅ 𝔐(L) ⊗ 𝔐(L′)`. For two G_K-stable lattices `L ⊂ L′` in the same crystalline representation, `𝔐(L) → 𝔐(L′)` is an isomorphism after inverting `p` and the two differ by a modification along `p = 0`.

**§7.22.** Rank one: invertible prismatic F-crystals on `Spf(O_K)` correspond to crystalline characters `G_K → Z_p^×`; `O_Δ{n}` ↔ `χ_cyc^n`. For a proper smooth formal scheme over `O_K` whose special fibre has torsion-free crystalline cohomology, the F-crystal `H^i(R f_* O_Δ)` corresponds to the lattice `H^i_ét(X_C, Z_p)` (the source's Example 4.6 with BMS1 Theorem 14.5). `K = Q_p`: End of the unit object is `Z_p` on both sides.

**§7.23.** `M = 𝔖`: `𝔖^{φ=1} = Z_p = (𝔖[1/u]^∧_p)^{φ=1}`. `K = Q_p(p^{1/p})`, `π = p^{1/p}`: `f =` 1/(u `− p`) lies in `𝔖⟨p/u^2⟩[1/u]` ∩ `A_inf[1/(q−1)]` and has a simple pole at `x_1`, so the set `B′` is nonempty (the source's Example 7.8); `B′` is empty when `e(K/K_0) < p`. The analogous statement for `A_inf` fails: `Hom(A_inf{1}, A_inf) = (μA_inf)^{φ=1} = 0` although both realisations are free of rank one over `Z_p`. Over `𝔖` the realisations of `𝔖{1}` and `𝔖` are `Z_p(1)` and `Z_p` restricted to `G_{K_∞}`, with no nonzero map between them; the proof uses that `u` does not divide a power of `q − 1` in `A_inf`.

**§7.24.** `D_𝔖(Z_p) = (𝔖, φ)`; `D_𝔖(Z_p(1)) = 𝔖{1}`, free of rank one with Frobenius a unit multiple of `E(u)^{-1}φ`. `D_𝔖` is not essentially surjective: an object of `Vect^φ(𝔖)` is in the image exactly when it carries a descent datum over `𝔖^{(1)}`. For `K = Q_p` and `π = p` versus `π′ = −p` the constructions use different coefficient embeddings; their base changes to `A_inf` agree. For the unit lattice both modules are `(Z_p[[u]],φ)`, so different uniformisers do not force different isomorphism classes.

**§7.25.** `L = Z_p(−1)`: `D_𝔖(L) = 𝔖{−1}`, of E-height 1, and Kisin's module of the lattice `Z_p(1)` of weight 1. p-divisible groups: for `G` over `O_K` the lattice `T_p(G)^∨` has Hodge–Tate weights in {−1, 0}, so `D_𝔖` of it has E-height at most 1; it is to be compared with the module of §R07.4.21 (`kisinPDivisibleClassification`), with its contravariant normalization: `V_𝔖(𝔐(G))=T_p(G)`. Its integral anti-equivalence holds for `p>2`; at `p=2`, §R07.4.21 supplies the isogeny classification. The two proofs of full faithfulness of restriction to `G_{K_∞}` (Kisin's and the prismatic one) have the same statement after inverting `p`.

**§7.26.** `p = 2`: `E = O_Δ{1}/2` has trivial realisation Z/2(1) `≅ Z/2` and is not trivial. With Z_p-coefficients the same twist `O_Δ{p−1}` is distinguished from `O_Δ` by its realisation `Z_p(p−1)`. The correct torsion and derived objects are expected to be perfect complexes on Drinfeld's stack `Σ″` (F-gauges), which also record the Nygaard filtration; their construction lies outside this roadmap.

### Dependencies

Layers 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6; AInfCohomology §§AI.2.1–AI.2.3, CrystallineCohomology §§CR.1.1–CR.1.3, CrystallineCohomology §§CR.4.1–CR.4.8, CR.DDR.4.5, CR.DDR.5.18–CR.DDR.5.22, DerivedModuleAlgebra §§1.1–1.9; DerivedDeRhamCohomology §§1.10–1.23, DerivedDeRhamCohomology §4.2; CrystallineCohomology §§CR.DDR.4.1, CR.DDR.4.3–CR.DDR.4.4, CR.DDR.5.8–CR.DDR.5.10, DerivedDeRhamCohomology §§5.1–5.7, DiamondsAndVStacks Layer D0, EnhancedDerivedSheaves E3, FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory R07.4, FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §R07.4.1 (`Sfrak`, `OE`, `toWittR`), FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §R07.4.14 (`crystallineRestrictionFullFaithfulness`), FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §R07.4.12 (`finiteHeightLattices`), FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §R07.4.10 (`kisinCrystallineEmbedding`), FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §R07.4.11 (`kisinEtaleFullFaithfulness`), FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §R07.4.2 (`KisinModule`), FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §R07.4.5 (`kummerEtalePhiModules`), FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory §R07.4.13 (`semistableFiniteHeight`), AInfCohomology §§AI.2.1–AI.2.3, `TauCetiRoadmap.PadicPeriodRings` §§1.5 and 3–4.3, FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory R07.3, FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory R07.4 §R07.4.7, PerfectoidSpaces P1.16–P1.18, P1.24–P1.25, PerfectoidSpaces P4.15.2–P4.15.3, PerfectoidSpaces P3, PerfectoidSpaces P3/finite-etale-tilting-equivalence, PerfectoidSpaces P3/finite-etale-under-completed-perfection, PerfectoidSpaces P3/fontaine-wintenberger, AdicEtaleGeometry A1.0.5 (henselian-finite-etale-approximation), §7.0 (absolute trace–prismatic comparison and Prismatic Beilinson square), SchemeAndStackFoundations §§1.1, 1.18–1.22, SchemeAndStackFoundations §§2.1–2.6, PrismaticCohomology §1.0; SchemeAndStackFoundations §§4.7–4.8, §7.0 (Filtered Frobenius bundle dictionary). Exact inputs are given in the supplier contracts; internal construction dependencies are linked above.

## Downstream consumers

PrismaticCohomologyPartIV uses the étale, syntomic and crystal descent theory. PadicHodgeTheory, CohomologyComparisons and HodgeTateAndCanonicalSubgroups consume the corresponding realizations and comparisons.

VectorBundlesAndIsocrystals extends the shared absolute curve package to general coefficient fields and relative bases. At `E=ℚ_p, F=C^♭`, its homogeneous eigensection ring, Proj curve, coherent and bundle comparisons, and annular Robba equivalence use §7.0's absolute carriers and maps. The filtered modification and `FilteredBundle.prismaticIdentification` remain Part III targets; their construction is not a prerequisite for the shared package.

## References

**Huber96.** R. Huber, *Étale Cohomology of Rigid Analytic Varieties and Adic Spaces*, Aspects of Mathematics E30, Vieweg, 1996. Corollary 3.2.2 and Theorem 3.2.9 use printed pages 174 and 180. The text is not open access; it is cited by locator only.

- **Kisin06**: Mark Kisin. *Crystalline representations and F-crystals*. In: Algebraic geometry and number theory, Progr. Math. 253, Birkhäuser, 2006, pp. 459–496. Lemma 1.2.2, Lemma 1.2.6, Proposition 1.2.8, Theorem 1.2.15 and Proposition 2.1.12. The text is not open access; it is cited by locator only.

- **BM-arc**: Bhargav Bhatt and Akhil Mathew. [The arc-topology](https://arxiv.org/abs/1807.04725v4). Duke Math. J. 170 (2021); arXiv:1807.04725v4, with its pagination (Corollary 6.17, p.51).

- **ALB**: Johannes Anschütz and Arthur-César Le Bras. [Prismatic Dieudonné theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/1907.10525v4). arXiv:1907.10525v4, with its pagination (Lemma 3.4.2, p.24).

- **Kedlaya05**: Kiran S. Kedlaya. [Slope filtrations revisited](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0504204v4). Doc. Math. 10 (2005), 447–525; arXiv:math/0504204v4, with its pagination (Definition 2.2.13, p.9; Lemma 2.3.5, p.12; Remark 2.3.8, p.13; Definition 2.4.8, p.15).

- **NS18**: Thomas Nikolaus and Peter Scholze. [On topological cyclic homology](https://arxiv.org/abs/1707.01799v2). Acta Math. 221 (2018), 203–409; arXiv:1707.01799v2, with its pagination (Corollary IV.4.16, p.130).

- **SW20**: Peter Scholze and Jared Weinstein. *Berkeley lectures on p-adic geometry*. Ann. of Math. Stud. 207, Princeton University Press, 2020; the authors' version is on the first author's web page. Definition 12.3.1 (integral Robba rings), Definition 13.4.3 (extended Robba rings) and Theorem 14.2.1 (extension of vector bundles over the closed point of `Spec A_inf`).

- **BL22**: Bhargav Bhatt and Jacob Lurie. [Absolute prismatic cohomology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2201.06120v1). arXiv:2201.06120v1, 16 January 2022.

- **BS23-FCRYS**: Bhargav Bhatt and Peter Scholze. [Prismatic F-crystals and crystalline Galois representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.14735v2). arXiv:2106.14735v2.

- **BMS2**: Bhargav Bhatt, Matthew Morrow and Peter Scholze. [Topological Hochschild homology and integral p-adic Hodge theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/1802.03261v2). arXiv:1802.03261v2, 9 April 2019.

- **BM23**: Bhargav Bhatt and Akhil Mathew. [Syntomic complexes and p-adic étale Tate twists](https://arxiv.org/abs/2202.04818v2). arXiv:2202.04818v2.

- **BMS1**: Bhargav Bhatt, Matthew Morrow and Peter Scholze. [Integral p-adic Hodge theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/1602.03148v3). arXiv:1602.03148v3, 16 January 2019.

- **AMMN22**: Benjamin Antieau, Akhil Mathew, Matthew Morrow and Thomas Nikolaus. [On the Beilinson fiber square](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2003.12541v2). arXiv:2003.12541; source version dated 29 September 2021; published in Duke Math. J. 171 (2022).

- **BS22**: Bhargav Bhatt and Peter Scholze. [Prisms and prismatic cohomology](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1905.08229v4). arXiv:1905.08229v4, 12 January 2022, 125 pages; author version.

- **ECD**: Peter Scholze. [Étale cohomology of diamonds](https://arxiv.org/abs/1709.07343v4). Version 4; printed pages.

- **FF**: Laurent Fargues and Jean-Marc Fontaine. *Courbes et fibrés vectoriels en théorie de Hodge p-adique*. Astérisque 406 (2018); printed book pages.

- **Lurie**: Jacob Lurie. [Rational and p-adic homotopy theory, DAG XIII](https://www.math.ias.edu/~lurie/papers/DAG-XIII.pdf). §2.2, especially Remark 2.2.7, p.35.

- **Mathew**: Akhil Mathew. [Faithfully flat descent of almost perfect complexes in rigid geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/1912.10968). Theorem 7.8, p.28, Definition 7.1 and Propositions 7.3–7.7, pp.27–28; Theorem 5.8, p.18, treats universal descent.

- **CS**: Kęstutis Česnavičius and Peter Scholze. [Purity for flat cohomology](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1912.10932v3). Theorem 5.3.5, pp.59–61, and Lemma 5.4.2, pp.63–66. Its §4.1.1 (pp.35–38) records the classification of finite locally free group schemes over perfect bases by Dieudonné modules due to Berthelot (Ann. Sci. École Norm. Sup. 13, 1980), Gabber (unpublished) and Lau (J. Amer. Math. Soc. 26, 2013).

- **Stacks**: [Tag 0D4B](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0D4B), effectivity of finite projective modules for adic completion.

The library interfaces use Mathlib commit `6b7abb3c7686292736be2955bd3eb9ebf63b456a` and Tau Ceti commit `a91d3aafa8cd3e6bc33dfde0d7677ed0f1625039`. Code declarations, rather than page numbers, locate these algebraic interfaces.
